Texas pounds Arkansas in home finale. Will the Longhorns play again in Austin this year?

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Following Saturday's dominating win over Arkansas, there was only one unanswered question for the Texas football team.

Will the Longhorns return to Royal-Memorial Stadium this season?

Arch Manning accounted for three touchdowns and Texas seized an early lead that it wouldn't relinquish in a 45-3 rout of the Razorbacks. With the 42-point victory on the UT campus, the Longhorns improved to 9-2 on the season and 6-2 in SEC play.

Arkansas was the last team on UT's home schedule this season. But Texas was ranked fifth in the College Football Playoff rankings this week, which is a notable number.

The fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth seeds will get to host a first-round playoff game in December, so there may be some additional action on Bevo Boulevard this year. A home playoff game isn't guaranteed, though. The Longhorns are still in contention for a first-round bye, and Texas could also slip in the rankings if it stumbles at Texas A&M next week.

Texas is now 6-1 at home this season. The Longhorns were last beaten at home during the month of November in 2022.

After Arkansas ended the first possession of Saturday's rivalry game with a punt, Texas put together a seven-play, 52-yard touchdown drive that was capped by a three-yard keeper from Manning. That early strike from UT was hardly a surprise, though. The Longhorns have now scored points on their first drive in eight games this season:

Ohio State: 1 play, -4 yards (lost fumble)

So, the first offensive drive was good. Again. But since UT averaged just 14.6 points in the first halves of its first 10 games this season, the bigger question was about how the Longhorns would look offensively on their second, third and fourth possessions.

More: Is Texas DB Jelani McDonald a Thorpe Award contender?

Texas turned its other three turns with the football during the first half into 10 points. The Longhorns punted before Gianni Spetic kicked his 18th field goal of the season, and Manning then closed out the first half with a 21-yard scoring strike to Emaree Winston and a 17-yard touchdown pass to Emmett Mosley V in the final three minutes of the second quarter. Add in a 31-yard interception return by Texas safety Jelani McDonald, and UT took a 31-3 lead into halftime.

That 28-point halftime lead was the largest of the Longhorns' season. Texas last took a lead of at least 28 points into the intermission during last year's game against Sam Houston.

Texas Longhorns quarterback Arch Manning (16) looks to pass as Texas takes on Arkansas at Darrell K Royal–Texas Memorial Stadium in Austin on Saturday, Nov. 21, 2026. (Mikala Compton/Austin American-Statesman)

There wasn't much suspense in the second half as Arkansas (4-7, 2-6) never cut into its 28-point halftime deficit. Backup running backs Michael Terry III and Derrek Cooper both scored for the Longhorns in the second half while the Razorbacks recorded two turnovers on downs and an interception inside of Texas territory.

While only playing in three quarters, Manning led the Longhorns with the 287 yards that he compiled while completing 21 of his 25 passes. Additionally, Manning was the last player that Texas recognized during its pregame senior day ceremony. Manning has not yet revealed if he'll enter the NFL Draft after this season, use his fifth year of eligibility and return to Texas or transfer elsewhere.

Texas star Colin Simmons sits out against Arkansas

Mosley finished with eight receptions and 106 receiving yards to pace the Texas receiving corps. Texas got six tackles apiece from Rasheem Biles, McDonald and Lance Jackson, whose older brother played at Arkansas.

Texas Longhorns head coach Steve Sarkisian watches a play under review in the second quarter of the Texas Longhorns' game against the Arkansas Razorbacks at Royal-Memorial Stadium in Austin, Nov. 21, 2026. (Sara Diggins/Austin American-Statesman)

With the postseason approaching, Texas will close out its regular season this upcoming Friday against Texas A&M. The Longhorns will travel to College Station for the 121st edition of this in-state rivalry. Texas is 2-0 against the Aggies since this rivalry resumed in 2024.

More: See the photos from the Texas-Arkansas football game

Texas A&M entered this weekend with a disappointing 4-6 record. The Aggies were playing No. 22 Oklahoma on Saturday afternoon.

In addition to trying to secure its spot in the College Football Playoff, Texas will also be contending next week for a spot in the SEC Championship Game. Due to tiebreaker rules, the Longhorns' potential spot in the SEC finale will also be dependent on how Alabama and Tennessee fare at the end of the regular season.

Washington stuns #8 Indiana, Jedd Fisch notches signature win

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Those are the three coaches that have beaten Curt Cignetti since he took over at Indiana. For the second time in three years, Washington has toppled the defending national champion, first in Fisch's first season at the helm when it downed Michigan.

The win over Indiana is significantly more impressive. The Hoosiers are not the juggernaut they were last season, but they are certainly a team capable of repeating as Big Ten champions and reaching the College Football Playoff. You don't beat Cignetti. It doesn't happen. Indiana's transformation is unlike anything college football has ever seen. A perennial loser, turning into the king of the sport. During that span, Washington has fallen from the national championship contender it was in 2023. Quarterback Demond Williams has gone from the heir to the throne of famed quarterbacks in Husky history to a polarizing student-athlete. Weekly, fans wonder if and when Fisch will no longer coach the team.

Never underestimate the power of music. Because as Husky Nation serenaded Fisch, his staff, and players with Prince's "Purple Rain" postgame, all the bad vibes washed away. Only hope remained.

How a game begins can tell you a lot about how it'll end. So as Williams raced down the sideline to open up the scoring in the first quarter with a 65-yard touchdown, Saturday instantly felt different than it had all season. Williams was aggressive as a runner, and as a thrower, he played with confidence, took and delivered contact, and ultimately never panicked, even though he was sacked four times and had an interception. Williams looked like the Prince who was promised, and though his fit for the throne has been widely debated since his transfer-portal entry in the offseason, it's clear that either Husky Nation must embrace him as king or watch another do it.

He finished with 227 passing yards, 88 rushing yards, and one touchdown through the air and on the ground. Jayden Limar added a rushing score along with 77 yards on 17 carries. Chris Lawson has carried Washington's receiver room for weeks. But Dezmen Roebuck was the top target, hauling in 10 catches for 92 yards. Decker DeGraaf had the lone receiving touchdown, hauling in the game-tying reception.

The Huskies got off to a nightmarish start defensively when Grant Wilson and Charlie Becker connected on a 74-yard touchdown on IU's first snap of the game. Huskies DC Ryan Walters' unit was great the rest of the way, containing both Wilson and Josh Hoover, who was inserted after being benched earlier this season. The biggest moment came on the final drive of the game, when UW kicker Tyler Robles nailed a 46-yard field goal to push UW up 27-24. Washington's defense had the game on its shoulders and answered by not allowing a first down. On fourth down, Indiana's attempt to tie the game went wide left.

Jedd Fisch has been highly criticized throughout his three years at the helm. Tonight, even his toughest critics are smiling. The victory over the Hoosiers gave Fisch a signature win, the most impressive of his head coaching career.

What's left on the schedule is a bout with Oregon on the road. The Ducks suddenly have a chance to make the Big Ten Championship Game due to Washington's win over Indiana. For Fisch, the opportunity to rip Oregon's heart out on the road and keep it out of the conference title game and the College Football Playoff is a golden one for his reputation and the program's momentum.

Houston football’s Keisean Henderson gets real in emotional postgame interview after big win in first career start

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Houston football's Keisean Henderson gets real in emotional postgame interview after big win in first career start appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

With Houston football starting quarterback Conner Weigman unable to play against West Virginia on Saturday, as he was ruled out right before the game, freshman Keisean Henderson came in and led the No. 20 Cougars to a 34-31 win in his first start. As the Houston football team was looking to bounce back after the Colorado upset from the week prior, Henderson provided a spark to help the Cougars win, as he was emotional after the game.

Coming into college football as the No. 4 overall recruit in ESPN's 2026 300 list, he would end up throwing for 268 yards, completing 18 of 28 passes to go along with a touchdown pass. Speaking to TNT Sports after the game, Henderson was noticeably emotional when talking about his teammates and coaches for believing in him.

"I'm just proud of myself and my team," Henderson said. "It's a brotherhood in here, man. They all believed in me through every moment. My team, my coaches, from Monday, they told me to get ready. But they always been telling me to get ready, and I told them my motto to myself is stay down."

40-year-old Josh Johnson making historic Cardinals start not seen since Jim Thorpe

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It is rarely a positive development for a team that it must start a fourth different quarterback in four weeks.

For the Cardinals, it is somewhat historic.

The quarterback-desperate Cardinals will turn to the ageless but also aged Josh Johnson on Sunday in Kansas City, where the longtime backup will play a game at 40 years and 191 days old.

Arizona Cardinals quarterback Josh Johnson leaves the field after an NFL football game against the Los Angeles Rams in Glendale, Ariz., Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Ross D. Franklin

According to Mark Dalton, the organization's senior vice president of media relations, Johnson will become the second-oldest non-specialist to play in a game for the Cardinals. To find the oldest, go all the way to Jim Thorpe in 1928, when the legend was 41 years and 185 days old in a Thanksgiving Day game against the Bears (which did not go well, with the Chicago Cardinals losing 34-0).

Johnson is getting a chance because Jacoby Brissett, who started in Week 8's win in Dallas, was traded to the Ravens; Gardner Minshew II, who started in Week 9's loss in Seattle, required season-ending elbow surgery; and rookie Carson Beck, who started in the Week 10 loss to the Rams, suffered back spasms that will not allow him to play Sunday.

So it is Johnson, who was signed off the Bengals' practice squad earlier this month, getting an opportunity to play a game for an eighth different franchise in a career that began as a fifth-round pick in 2008.

Football player Jim Thorpe poses for a photo between 1910 and 1920. HUM Images/Universal Images Grou

"There is no next step to the playbook, this is going back to the starting table of contents, and that's OK," head coach Mike LaFleur said, according to the team's website. "For a three-and-a-half-hour window, it's my job to figure out as you know whatever we can to get the ball moving forward."

Johnson will not become the oldest active player in the NFL. That title belongs to Aaron Rodgers, who turns 43 on Dec. 2 and will start for the Steelers in Philadelphia on Sunday.

‘His talent is bigger than this performance’ – De Zerbi hails goalscorer Williams-Barnett

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Roberto De Zerbi believes Luca Williams-Barnett's "talent is bigger than this performance" after the 18-year-old impressed on his first start for Tottenham against Ipswich Town.

Williams-Barnett tucked away Pedro Porro's cross in the 15th minute to net his first goal for Spurs, sending the home fans into raptures in north London.

Further chances fell to Mohammed Kudus and Jan Paul van Hecke, before Dominic Solanke struck in the 51st minute to cap off a move that once again involved Williams-Barnett, sealing a 2-0 victory for Spurs.

At 18 years and 51 days old, Williams-Barnett became the youngest player to score for Tottenham in the Premier League since Gareth Bale struck against Fulham in September 2007 (18y 47d).

And his performance has certainly left a big impression on De Zerbi, who had previously labelled the starlet "one of the best young players I have ever worked with."

"He's played very well, but his talent is bigger than this performance," De Zerbi told BBC Match of the Day.

"It was the first game, and I am very happy. I am very delighted with his performance, but he is a big, big, big talent.

"The management of the younger talented players is different because you can't take risks to lose their enthusiasm and their positive mentality.

"But I think Tottenham's dressing room now is a good environment for him.

"I put James [Maddison] to play very close to him to speak the same language but also to help him and put him in the best conditions to play. To play with freedom, to play with his qualities because his qualities are very big.

"He's a mature guy. He is smart and understands the situation. He has great behaviour inside the group and the group is good enough to help him stay calm and to play without pressure, because he has to enjoy it on the pitch.

"He has to work hard but also he has to keep the passion to keep the love of football."

“I am happy because he played with enjoyment, with passion, without pressure.”  Roberto De Zerbi was full of praise for Luca Williams-Barnett after today's win!

— Tottenham Hotspur (@SpursOfficial) November 21, 2026

Williams-Barnett (18y 51d) became the sixth-youngest player to start a Premier League game for Tottenham, after Stephen Carr (17y 28d), Mikey Moore (17y 77d), Andy Turner (17y 145d), Philip Ifil (17y 270d) and Gareth Bale (18y 41d).

The youngster also became the fourth-youngest player to score on his first Premier League start, behind Daniel Jebbison in May 2021 (17y 276d), Danny Cadamarteri in September 1997 (17y 343d), and Mark Cullen in May 2010 (18y 12d).

Ipswich boss Gary O'Neil, meanwhile, admitted that his side were not at their best at Tottenham Hotspur Stadium.

Indeed, Ipswich are now winless across their last five top-flight games, and they have conceded in each of their last 30 Premier League matches.

"We gave them a really soft first goal, which obviously gives them some comfort," O'Neil said.

"And then we didn't get to our level enough today. We got into some good attacking situations, but we didn't show enough quality.

"We should have dealt with both goals better. So all in all, we can't argue with the result. They were better than us which we have to accept."

Christensen and Cubarsi injuries hand Barca boss Flick defensive headache

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Hansi Flick has been left with a defensive headache ahead of this weekend's clash with Villarreal, with both Andreas Christensen and Pau Cubarsi suffering from injuries.

Flick confirmed Christensen would miss the next two matches after sustaining a calf issue in training, having only just returned from an injury lay-off earlier in the month.

The Danish defender played 30 minutes of Barcelona's 2-1 win over Atletico Madrid before the international break, but his absence offers Flick a problem he must try and tackle.

Cubarsi is another player likely to miss out on Sunday's clash after he was injured during Spain's 3-2 defeat to England in the Nations League, with the 19-year-old replaced at half-time having given away a penalty on Harry Kane in the first half.

"Andreas was really in a good moment, so this is why the decision was made not to go with the national team so he could train here," Flick said of Christensen's injury.

"I think it was the right decision, but unfortunately he's out for maybe two games. He is really disappointed.

"For me, when fit, he's one of the best central defenders. We give him our support and also we have to help him to maybe improve a little physically."

When asked about Cubarsi's issue, Flick added: "It's not a big thing, but we have to take care. He could be on the bench [vs. Villarreal] and we will see if he travels to Baku.

"With Cuba, he played amazingly last season, but with the World Cup he had a lot of minutes, and he's only 19 years old. So, it's now about taking care of him."

The man in charge. https://t.co/VJJNEdUFzY

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) November 21, 2026

It remains to be seen who Flick will deploy in his reshuffled back line, with Xavi Espart, who signed a new long-term contract earlier this week, also out with an injury.

Joules Kounde and Eric Garcia are, however, available again after recovering from knocks, though Flick does have a more unconventional solution in Frenkie de Jong.

De Jong impressed at right-back during Barcelona's win over Atletico, and the Blaugrana boss hinted the Netherlands international could be utilised there again.

"We will see," Flick added. "I am very happy with Frenkie. He felt something [with the national team] but it was a small injury and he is available."

Higher up the pitch, Dani Olmo is set for his first appearance since October after recovering from an injury, but Rodri remains out with a hamstring problem.

"We have to wait day by day, but I think it's a good moment," Flick said of Rodri's fitness. "We also take care about this because, last season with Manchester City, he had a similar injury.

"I spoke this morning with him, and it took a little bit longer than they expected, so now we have to take care. It's not the same injury, it's not the big injury like he had in the past, but I think we have to take care."

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) November 21, 2026

Moreno has been involved in five goals in 14 LaLiga appearances against Barcelona (three goals, two assists), scoring or assisting in his last two.

Despite being favoured in the pre-match simulations, Barcelona have lost two of their last five LaLiga matches against Villarreal (W3), twice as many defeats as they suffered in their previous 29 meetings with them in the competition (W22 D6 L1).

Indeed, after a run of 10 consecutive home wins against Villarreal in LaLiga between 2010 and 2020, Barcelona have lost three of their last five home games against them in the competition (W2).

Since the 2021-22 season, Barcelona have not suffered more home defeats in LaLiga against any other opponent than they have against Villarreal (P5 W2 D0 L3), also three against Real Madrid (P6 W3 D0 L3).

Barcelona have, however, earned 33 points from 12 games in the 2026-27 LaLiga season (W11 L1). Counting three points for a win, they have only accumulated more points at this stage of the competition in the 2012-13, 2013-14 and 2017-18 seasons (34 points – W11 D1), also earning 33 points in the 2024-25 season (W11 L1).

Flick's side have also outperformed their expected goals by more than any other LaLiga side this season (+8.4, with 48 goals from 39.6 xG), while Villarreal have underperformed theirs by the most (-7.4, with 17 goals from 24.4 xG).

And they face a Villarreal side that have picked up seven points from their last four LaLiga games (W2 D1 L1), just one point fewer than they managed in their first eight games (eight points – P8 W2 D2 L4).

Real Madrid injury, suspension list and return dates vs. PSV Eindhoven: Rodrygo, Ferland Mendy, Raul Asencio updates

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Adeyeye Oluwapelumi | 21 Nov 2026 23:59, Last updated: 22 Nov 2026 00:02

Looking to extend their perfect record in the Champions League campaign,  Real Madrid welcome PSV Eindhoven to the Santiago Bernabeu on Tuesday night for Matchday Five of the league phase.

Los Blancos, who have won each of their four continental outings this term, have now recorded victories in nine games in a row across all competitions, most recently coming from behind to beat Celta Vigo 2-1 on Saturday to remain second in the La Liga table.

Bernardo Silva and Aurelien Tchouameni were both available after fitness concerns during the international break, while Vinicius Junior returned from his four-match ban, though Mourinho still has several players unavailable through injury.

Here, Sports Mole rounds up Real Madrid's latest injury and suspension news ahead of their meeting with PSV, who travel to the Spanish capital with four points from their opening four Champions League matches.

Rodrygo continues his recovery from an ACL injury and has yet to return to first-team training, with the Brazil international not expected to be back until the start of 2027.

Ferland Mendy had been expected to return against Celta Vigo but was left out of the squad as he continues his recovery from a thigh injury.

The Frenchman is also ineligible to feature against PSV on Tuesday after being omitted from Real Madrid's Champions League squad for the league phase.

Raul Asencio underwent surgery for a stress fracture in his right tibia on October 1 and has made encouraging progress in his recovery, having returned to grass training and ball work.

However, Tuesday's clash with PSV is expected to come too soon for the Spanish defender.

Real Madrid have no suspended players for this match.

Roberto De Zerbi compares Luca Williams-Barnett to Lamine Yamal after Tottenham win

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Williams-Barnett scored after 15 minutes and then set up Dominic Solanke

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Tottenham boss Roberto De Zerbi was not surprised by a dazzling full debut display from “special” talent Luca Williams-Barnett and compared the 18-year-old to Barcelona’s Lamine Yamal after Saturday’s 2-0 win over Ipswich.

De Zerbi turned to the highly-rated Spurs academy graduate after two bright cameos and he delivered with aplomb, scoring on his first Premier League start after 15 minutes before a mazy run set up Dominic Solanke at the start of the second half.

After Williams-Barnett made his senior debut at the age of 16 last season, the clamour for the teenager to play has only increased during a difficult last 12 months and De Zerbi decided 10 days ago to include him in the starting line-up against Ipswich.

During a chat on Thursday with the skilful England Under-19 international, De Zerbi asked Williams-Barnett to play with freedom and work hard without the ball “like Raphinha, like Yamal” before he agreed when asked if the teenager could reach similar heights.

After De Zerbi revealed Williams-Barnett prepared for the match on the team bus by watching YouTube clips of Ronaldinho, Eden Hazard and Neymar, he said: “Luca is a special player.

“I’m not surprised, because his talent is bigger than you think. He played very well. I think he’s lucky, because he’s playing in a top club, with top team-mates.

“I decided to play today with Luca from 10 days and in my plan, it was (James) Maddison and Luca very close to speak the same language, to play together and the experience of Maddison I thought could be important.

“The behaviour of Maddison could help him to play with freedom. And Thursday, I spoke with Luca and I asked him two things. Just two things. To play like he plays on the park with his friends, with the same freedom.

“And to play and to work hard without the ball, like (Ousmane) Dembele, like Raphinha, like Yamal, because football is going in a different way and the first defenders are the attackers.

“Even if they are Ballon d’Or winners, or any way the players with talent, they have to work on the pitch.”

Asked if Williams-Barnett can reach the level of Yamal, De Zerbi said: “Yes, I think so.

“And one of the best things in my career… I’ve seen today in the bus from the training ground to the stadium, because he was very close with me, while we were coming in the stadium, he was watching on YouTube Ronaldinho, Hazard, Neymar.

“I thought he was a special guy, because if one hour and thirty minutes before you are debuting in the Premier League, you are watching Ronaldinho, Hazard, Neymar, all these types of talent. The football is magic, no?”

Gary O’Neil watched Ipswich’s winless run extend to five matches.

“Many of us weren’t quite able to get to the level that we’re used to. A tough afternoon. I didn’t enjoy it but I did enjoy watching the lads dig in and find a way to keep going together in a tough moment because that’s what we need,” O’Neil said.

“Between the goals and after the goals, we were OK and we arrived in the final third enough to cause problems but we didn’t. Disappointing but it was a big task coming here and we needed things to be perfect.”

Mikel Arteta provides David Raya injury update: ‘Never good news’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Noni Madueke rescued a point for the champions in a 2-2 draw at Newcastle but Raya was forced off injured

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Mikel Arteta said that goalkeeper David Raya will undergo an MRI scan on his injured shoulder as the Gunners boss lamented his side dropping two points following a 2-2 draw at Newcastle.

Eberechi Eze put Arsenal ahead just past the half hour and he saw a free-kick saved on the stroke of half-time after Noni Madueke spurned a couple of chances to put the visitors further in front.

But Newcastle, booed off at the break, rallied on the resumption and Martin Odegaard being dispossessed from a throw-in allowed Harvey Barnes to score while Dan Burn’s free header from a corner caused a goalmouth scramble and Malick Thiaw capitalised to poke home from a yard out.

Arsenal then lost goalkeeper Raya to injury, although they claimed a share of the spoils thanks to Madueke 12 minutes from time as the Premier League leaders moved six points clear of Brighton, who could halve the deficit when they play at Hull on Sunday.

Arteta said: “My feeling is we have lost two points. We were exceptional in the first half from the beginning to the end. We should have been two, three or four-nil up clearly.

“When you don’t do that then the opponent is going to try to react and right from the beginning (of the second half) we weren’t at the races. One thing is to give them belief and another thing is to give them a goal. We did both.

“After that, we came back and we were exceptional. We scored the second goal and we had opportunities to go and kill the game. We lacked a moment of magic to be able to do that.

“It’s a pity because the margin should have been much bigger for the level we showed. I never remember that we played at that level here, never. We have won football matches playing much worse than we did today here.”

Raya was forced off in the 70th minute, having received treatment when he slumped to the floor after coming out to collect a corner, and left the field clutching his arm with an apparent shoulder problem.

Arteta said: “Unfortunately, he had to come off, which is never good news. He will get assessed now and he will probably have an MRI tomorrow to see what it is exactly because at the moment, I don’t know.”

Bruno Guimaraes also made way when Raya was withdrawn, with the former Newcastle midfielder’s every touch heavily barracked on his first return to Tyneside since his £75m move in the summer.

Arteta, though, spoke favourably of the Brazilian’s performance, adding: “It’s never easy, emotionally, you have so much going on, especially the way he feels connected to this club. But I thought he had a very good performance.”

Matthias Jaissle reflected on a performance of two halves – but Newcastle’s head coach hopes they can take their second 45-minute display into the rest of the season.

The German said: “It was pretty clear in the first half we were not that front-foot or that intense, somehow hesitating to make the last step or last yard.

“This is something you can’t afford against a top team. But the second half was pure belief, pure conviction, no hesitation any more, pure trust between the team-mates that they are doing their jobs.

“This is the Newcastle DNA and I hope we can build on the second half.”

Arsenal face nervous wait with David Raya set for scan after being forced off against Newcastle... as club legend Ian Wright admits he's 'petrified' of losing the keeper

📅 2026-11-21  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 16:27 EST, 21 November 2026 | Updated: 19:02 EST, 21 November 2026

Arsenal will be keeping everything crossed ahead of goalkeeper David Raya undergoing tests on what appeared to be a shoulder or arm injury sustained against Newcastle.

The Gunners' No1 went off in the second half in the 2-2 draw at St James' Park, supporting his arm or shoulder before he was replaced by back-up Kepa Arrizabalaga.

It leaves everyone associated with Arsenal nervous, as they face potentially a sustained period of time without a key part of their impressive defence.

Raya appeared to pick up the injury by running into Newcastle's Will Osula after claiming the ball from a cross.

As he attempted to run forward to start an attack, he collided with the striker and hit the ground in pain.

After a brief period of treatment on the field, he was substituted, supporting his arm, and replaced in the game.

"I'm petrified!" Ian Wright and Jamie Redknapp on why David Raya coming off injured vs Newcastle is a massive worry for Arsenal 🧤 pic.twitter.com/F5uAgjFdp0

Arsenal face a nervy wait to find out the extent of David Raya's injury picked up against Newcastle on Saturday

Manager Mikel Arteta has been left sweating at the idea of being without his No1 for a busy Christmas period.

'Unfortunately, he had to come off, which is not good news,' Arteta said: 'He will have an MRI assessment tomorrow, but at the moment I don't know.

'It was a knock, I don't know how it happened and what the injury is at the moment. We have to wait and see tomorrow.

'We know how important David is for us. Tomorrow we will have more news.'

The Gunners will play nine more games before the end of the year as they look to push forward in both the league and Champions League.

That challenge has just got harder, though, and Arsenal fan and former forward Ian Wright has expressed his concern.

'That's a massive worry,' he said on Sky Sports. 'I don't want to say I'm petrified simply because Kepa is in goal, he's the guy who has got to try and keep the goals out for us now.

'But yeah, that is a massive blow. Someone like him, Declan Rice, Gabriel, it is very tough to replace those guys.'

Arsenal are next in action against Borussia Dortmund in the Champions League on Tuesday, before a crunch clash with Manchester City in the Premier League next Sunday.

Forget 'Landman,' Billy Bob Thornton's Netflix Sports Drama Is a Masterpiece

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sports can be a difficult thing to capture on film. The raw passion of the crowd, the elation or despair of the result, and the personal histories we have with our teams are rarely dramatized in a way that is satisfying for those who get it. But in 2004, Friday Night Lights captured that feeling. The story of a high school football team fighting off-field struggles on its way to glory was a box office hit, inspiring a long-running TV spin-off. The reason for this longevity is a movie that combines an authentic performance from Billy Bob Thornton, excellent direction from director Peter Berg, and a script that understands just what it means to love America’s Game.

Welcome to Permian High School, an institution in Odessa, Texas, known across the community for one thing – its football team, the Permian Panthers. The coach, Gary Gaines (Thornton), is tasked with taking the team to the state championship, with elite running back James "Boobie" Miles (Derek Luke) their key to victory. However, a mix-up during a low-stakes game sees Miles get injured, throwing the season into jeopardy and piling pressure on Gaines. Off the field, the teenage athletes cope with personal problems, family issues, and the kind of pressure normally reserved for pro players. It’s up to Gaines to pull them together through the adversity, and learn how to win as a team again.

The movie is based on the real-life Permian Panthers during their 1988 season, and the real Miles himself makes a cameo as a coach. The film was a hit that led to a spin-off TV show that ran for five seasons between 2006 and 2011, which focused on another fictional town in the same state. There’s something about the original movie, however, that stands out in the crowded genre of sports films.

What happens when the hopes and dreams of a whole town rely on a group of teenage boys who haven’t begun to figure out who they are yet? That’s the question at the heart of a movie where the action ramps up when the game ends. Berg looks at the fragile nature of dreams, particularly in the world of football, where someone’s future hangs on the perfect kick, the right result, or staying injury-free. The team succeeds and fails, but the nuance of the story never gives any straightforward conclusions. The players’ personal issues are still there when they win, and losing doesn’t define who they are.

The collective passion of the parents, alumni, and staff is also a mixed blessing. On one hand, their support offers a platform for these young men to achieve their dreams. But on the other, it means Gaines dealing with numerous local figures becoming deeply invested in outcomes they cannot control, and holding the group responsible for more than just a score. It’s a uniquely American story, where an extracurricular activity can hold the hearts of an entire town, and the movie does a great job of showing the negative sides of that obsession, as well as the exhilarating highs that fuel it.

One might be tempted to portray a football coach as a hot-blooded control freak, throwing things across the locker room and giving inspirational talks to their charges. What makes Thornton’s portrayal unique is that Gaines is nothing like that stereotype, but at the same time, we’re never in doubt of the emotions lying beneath the surface. Gaines must face the responsibility of being the coach, and being held accountable for the fury of the fanbase, but at the same time protect this group of teenagers both as athletes and as people. His joy, his frustration, and his candor are delivered subtly, as he asks his team: “Can you live in that moment as best you can, with clear eyes, and love in your heart, with joy in your heart? If you can do that, gentleman, you're perfect!”

Helping him achieve that perfection is a cast of future stars, including Jay Hernandez (Suicide Squad, Magnum P.I.), Taylor Kitsch (Battleship, John Carter), and Lucas Black (the Fast and Furious franchise). These up-and-comers make their roles more than jock archetypes, instead letting the audience in on an adolescence that forces them to handle enormous pressure — in many cases without the proper guidance to deal with it.

If the ups and downs of real-life football aren’t providing enough adrenaline, Friday Night Lights provides the stakes and the personnel to make you a fan. It’s no wonder that this 2000s hit is still popular on Netflix, as the themes it covers are timeless.

Antonelli Takes Pole Position in Las Vegas, Verstappen Inherits P1 with Grid Penalty

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A cold qualifying session saw the contenders wait until the final second to take their laps, with Antonelli winning the waiting game.

Kimi Antonelli took pole for the Las Vegas Grand Prix by being the last of the contenders to start their final flying lap in Q3. With a five-place grid drop, the new F1 champion will start Saturday night’s Grand Prix from sixth.

Max Verstappen, who held provisional pole until the final flurry of attempts, will inherit the P1 starting spot, and George Russell will start second after qualifying third.

Russell, by far the fastest in Friday’s practice, asked the Mercedes pit wall if he could have a tow, knowing Mercedes was the clear frontrunner and his champion teammate was destined to start further back in the field.

Mercedes didn’t oblige, and both drivers entered the waiting game with the rest of the contenders, as no one wanted to be the first driver out for the final lap, punching through the frigid cold air of the Las Vegas desert alone.

Ultimately, Verstappen went first and did not improve on his first Q3 time, which was still good enough for an elevated front-row starting spot.

Lando Norris will start third after qualifying fourth ahead of Isack Hadjar and Arvid Lindblad. Oscar Piastri found himself in a familiar starting position of seventh but faces a 10-place grid penalty.

With rain on Thursday and a dry forecast for every session of consequence during the Las Vegas Grand Prix weekend, most contenders first saw what their new 2027 cars had during FP3 on Friday, ahead of qualifying.

Neither Ferrari made it to Q3, settling for 11th and 12th as neither Lewis Hamilton nor Charles Leclerc put themselves in position to give the other a tow.

Nico Hulkenberg, Carlos Sainz, Gabriel Bortoleto, and Alex Albon were knocked out in Q2.

Haas reserve driver Rafael Camara nearly made it to Q2 in his second F1 qualifying attempt but was knocked out at the last minute by the Audis. He joined Sergio Perez, Fernando Alonso, Lance Stroll, Valtteri Bottas, and Ollie Bearman.

Victoria Beaver is a nomadic sports writer who spends her time hopping between race tracks and hippie farms. She’s covered every corner of motorsports that will let her in from 410 Sprints to NASCAR to Supercross. Her daily driver is a 2010 Subaru that she refused to do the smallest amount of preventative maintenance on. Instead, she spends her free time and money building a 42-foot Skoolie to one day travel the country full time.

James Harden’s new role, same old burst: A 51-point night that moved him past Kobe Bryant

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

CLEVELAND (AP) — James Harden's game has evolved over the past couple seasons where he is counted on more for getting open shots for teammates than being the prolific scorer he was during his first 12 years in the league.

On Friday night though, the Cleveland Cavaliers guard showed that he can still have games that leave fans and teammates in awe.

Harden scored 51 points and passed Kobe Bryant for third-most, 50-point games in NBA history as the Cavaliers defeated the Indiana Pacers 128-119 in an NBA Cup game.

"It's a different role for me. My usage rate isn't extremely high every game. We have somebody for that. So mine is kind of scoring when I need to, but then distributing the ball," Harden said. "My number is called and you need to go out there and put the shots up. Ok, I can still do it. I knew I can do it, but it's just when you don't do something consistently and then it goes away a little bit or you just don't know if you got it or not."

Harden is at 26 50-point games after being tied with Bryant for nearly year. His last big game was nearly a year ago with the Los Angeles Clippers, when he scored 55 against Charlotte on Nov. 22, 2025.

Michael Jordan is second with 31 while Wilt Chamberlain is the runaway leader with 118.

"When you talk about just basketball, obviously Wilt was elite, but you got Mike and Kobe, shooting guard position, the best to ever do it. So just to be in that conversation, it's very humbling and it's definitely special," Harden said.

The performance by Harden was the type of game the 18-year veteran had during a run with the Houston Rockets, when he was named the NBA MVP during the 2017-18 season, and had three straight seasons where he averaged at least 30 points a game.

Harden played a season-high 40 minutes was 15 of 26 from the field, including 7 of 11 from beyond the arc. He also made all 14 of his free throws and had six rebounds and six assists for the Cavaliers, who have won three of four but only 9-8 after reaching the Eastern Conference finals last season.

"Nights like that are so special. The crowd appreciated that performance. That wasn't like we're 10 games above .500 and everything's peachy. So it was a clutch performance. We needed that tonight," coach Kenny Atkinson said.

Harden, who turned 37 in August, also became the sixth NBA player age 37 or older to have a 50-point game, joining Bryant, Jordan, LeBron James, Jamal Crawford and Steph Curry.

Harden said he felt like it was going to be a big night from the opening tip. He scored 15 in the first quarter and was up to 24 at halftime.

"That was vintage where really you can't do anything if you're the opposing coach. Especially when he's getting by you like that, when they started taking away a step back and one or two crosses and he's in the lane, then there's just no stopping him," Atkinson said. "There's really nothing you can do. So vintage, very reminiscent of his Houston days."

Harden had 42 points after three quarters when the Cavaliers were up 103-82. Indiana made a run to get it within six with two minutes remaining when Harden made one final finishing push. A 3-pointer from the top of the key with 57.9 seconds left extended Cleveland's lead to 124-113 before he made a pair of free throws to get over 50 15 seconds later.

"I think what excites me a lot is even being in my 18th year learning how to still be impactful. It's easy when you're young, 23, 24, you run around, you jump high and can move fast and get by," he said, "Now it's like, you're not as fast. Can you still give by people or how can you get by somebody? That's the evolution of James Harden and figuring that out. Tonight was a confidence booster that I can do it at a high level still."

Harden also became the sixth player in Cavs history to have a 50-point game, and the first since Donovan Mitchell's 71-point outing on Jan. 2, 2023, against Chicago.

"I think I can speak for every hooper, especially when you're at that level. Sometimes you just need that. Sometimes you need to just remind yourself just because you're in such a rare era doesn't mean you're not human. And I think for him to tell you guys that, I'm glad he said that because that shows the human side that we all see of him," Mitchell said.

→ Baca artikel asli

Sabb, Glassboro dominate second half to earn return to state final

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

WEST DEPTFORD — It's been a tried and true formula that has enabled Glassboro's football team to make the state championship game its permanent address. The Bulldogs are known for their big-play offense and tenacious defense, and the combination earned them a fourth straight trip to a state championship game.

Xavier Sabb led the big play offensive with two touchdowns and defensive end Maurice Davis Jr. paced a defensive effort that pitched a shutout after the opening drive as Glassboro defeated West Deptford, 33-7 in a state Group 2 semifinal game.

Glassboro (12-1), the two-time defending Group 1 champion, will face Caldwell in next Saturday's 1 p.m. Group 2 state final at MetLife Stadium.

This is the fifth year that the NJSIAA has held public school state playoffs. In 2023, Glassboro lost in the Group 1 state finals and then won it the past two seasons.

"I think this is going to be the best team we had in history," said Sabb, a key member of the previous teams that reached the state final.

West Deptford had scored on the game's opening drive, with Cole Paskiewicz (18 carries, 47 yards) capping a 10-play, 64 yards drive with a 1-yard touchdown run.

Then Sabb returned the ensuing kickoff 97-yards for a touchdown. At first, it appeared as if the kick would go out of bounds, but at the last second, Sabb decided to pick it up and then he darted down the left side for the touchdown.

"Realistically, I thought the ball was going out into the end zone, so I let it go and then I picked it up late and saw a gap and took it all the way through," said Sabb, an Oregon commit. "All my teammates came and blocked for me, and without my teammates I never would have made it."

What is interesting is that Glassboro ran just one play from scrimmage in the first quarter. The Bulldogs didn't do much better in the second and trailed 7-6 at halftime.

Coach Timmy Breaker, now in his fourth season at his alma mater, made the proper halftime adjustments.

"We changed a couple of things at halftime," Breaker said. "On film when they ran the veer it was to the A gap," he said, referring to the area right by the center. "So when they came out, they changed it and ran to the B gap, the C gap and widened it, so we adjusted to the veer to their formations."

West Deptford threw for 36 yards, so once Glassboro stopped the run, the Eagles didn't have any answers.

Glassboro had the first possession of the second half and following a short West Deptford kick, the Bulldogs began pounding the ball. Columbia commit JoJo DeLeece ran three straight times for 29 yards and then Davon Barr, in his first carry of the game, finished matters with a 16-yard scoring run.

"It was an inside zone and I saw that we had numbers, and I saw No. 8 (tight end Riley Brown-Dispensa) so I ran by him and scored the touchdown," Barr said.

Glassboro scored on its next possession as a scrambling Charlie Foulke found Ausar Heard wide open in the end zone for a 25-yard scoring pass. Foulke then hit receiver Mekhi Parker for the two-point conversion and the Bulldogs had a 20-7 lead with 4:30 left in the third quarter.

Meanwhile, the defense was frustrating Deptford. Leading the way was Davis, a 6-foot-5, 235-pound senior, who had a sack, a tackle for loss and a fumble recovery.

"Maurice did an exceptional job and held his own tonight," Breaker said about Davis.

Late in the quarter Glassboro got the ball on the West Deptford 35 after Davis recovered a fourth down fumble. On the next play, the Bulldogs scored when wide receiver Parker threw an option pass to Sabb for a 35-yard touchdown strike.

According to local historian Chuck Langerman, Sabb now has 404 career points and began the fifth Glassboro player to score 400 or more in his career. The others are Corey Clement (588 points), Terrance Holmes (472 points), Amari Saab (442 points) and Sean Redman (422 points).

Sabb had three receptions for 42 yards, but he made three huge plays — the kickoff return, the 35-yard touchdown and an interception and long return that set up Glassboro final TD.

Parker, among the best juniors in the state, then ended Glassboro's scoring by hauling in a seven-yard TD pass from Foulke.

A Syracuse commit, Foulke completed 7-of-14 passes for 86 yards and despite missing the first three games due to NJSIAA transfer rules since he played at St. Joseph's Prep in Philadelphia last year, Foulke has thrown for 2,052 yards. According to Langerman, Foulke is the second person in school history to throw for 2,000 yards in a season after Jack O'Connell threw for 2,737 last year.

West Deptford (12-1) has really turned the corner in three seasons under John Emel. The Eagles went 3-8 the year before his arrival and then were 5-5, 9-2 before this season when they won the highly competitive WJFL Constitutional Division and the South Jersey Group 2 championship.

"I don't think a team can play them any better than we did in Group 2," Emel said about Glassboro. "I think they're going to ceremoniously go up north and it's just a matter of what the final score is going to be."

Emel also realized that Sabb altered the entire game. After that opening kickoff, West Deptford wouldn't kick or punt the ball to Sabb, but short kicks gave Glassboro great field position.

"He's going to Oregon for a reason, he's a five-star player and Gatorade Player of the Year last year," Emel said. "I have a ton of respect for their players. Their players are really good."

Marc Narducci is a freelance reporter for the Courier-Post. He can be reached by email at cpvarsity.com

Gritty road trip was enough to give Knicks their mojo back

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Knicks left for their four-game road trip a bit unsettled, with questions circling them due to a rocky start to the season. After going 3-1 on the trip and beating potential playoff teams in the Raptors (twice) and Timberwolves, they return as a side rounding back into form and a better team than when they left.

"I feel like we're getting into a good rhythm offensively, defensively," Josh Hart said after the team's 102-99 win over the Timberwolves on Thursday night in Minneapolis. "I think you'll see some of the defensive rotations were on point. Obviously, we can be better. But we're continuing to learn, gain experience. Every year is different, every team is different. So I think we're doing that, and I think we're getting better.

"Whenever you win in the league, you continue to build confidence."

It is notable that they won in different ways: through free-flowing offensive showings; through grittier defensive efforts; and on Thursday, a combination of both. None of the three wins were blowouts — each game was on the line in crunch time.

New York Knicks center Karl-Anthony Towns looks on after an NBA basketball game against the Minnesota Timberwolves in Minneapolis, Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

"It shows that we're able to win in different ways," Hart said. "And that's what you have to do in the league. Some days, you're gonna shoot the cover off the ball and you can win by 10, 15, 20. Other games, it might be a possession game and offensively it's not really clicking, not knocking down shots, but you're getting stops defensively and you're able to pull out stops like that. I think it shows that this team can win games in different fashions. And that's the most important thing in the league."

A road trip like this can be beneficial, particularly early in the year as a new group gels. Despite all the continuity the Knicks have, they have been adamant that chemistry has to be reestablished.

They begin a five-game homestand Saturday, in which they'll host the surprisingly strong Trail Blazers, get a rematch with the Pacers (who could have back injured Tyrese Haliburton), play the Cavaliers for the third time already this season, see Giannis Antetokounmpo and the Heat for the first time and square off with the Hawks, who have started this season well after the Knicks eliminated them from the first round of the playoffs.

"For us to have a great road trip like this and win in so many different ways, it [says] a lot about our team," Karl-Anthony Towns said. "It's great when you can find a way to win. At the end of the day, it's hard to win in the NBA. I've learned that. It's hard to win. And you just have to find a way to win every single night. For us on this road trip to continue to find ways to win, it speaks to this team, and it's the most important thing when you play."

The Knicks' injury-free run came to an end.

New York Knicks forward OG Anunoby dunks during the first half of an NBA basketball game against the Minnesota Timberwolves, Thursday, Nov. 19, 2026, in Minneapolis. AP Photo/Ellen Schmidt

Jordan Clarkson (right plantar fasciitis) is out for Saturday's game against the Trail Blazers at Madison Square Garden. OG Anunoby (left calf tightness) is listed as questionable.

They might also get a break as Blazers star Deni Avdija is listed as questionable with a left foot contusion. He has missed the Blazers' last two games. Ja Morant remains out with a hamstring injury.

French team: the latest story from Kylian Mbappé

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kylian Mbappé decided to respond to criticism in his own way. While his withdrawal from the French team due to injury is being talked about, the Real Madrid striker published a story on Instagram in which we see him boarding a plane, dressed in the official Blues tracksuit, with Italy as an itinerary.

A publication which comes after his replacement in the French team group by Senny Mayulu. Zinédine Zidane had also confirmed his withdrawal on Wednesday at a press conference, on the eve of the Blues’ trip to Italy to face the Nazionale this Thursday evening in the League of Nations. Without speaking directly about the criticism of which he is the subject, the French captain thus displays his trip with the colors of the selection, despite his unavailability for this meeting.

Manu Tournoux is a passionate and knowledgeable football enthusiast with a special focus on French football. Bayern Munich, Vincent Kompany: “I don’t want to minimize the facts, it was a shock”

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

On October 23, two days after the draw conceded in the Champions League against Arsenal (2-2), Bayern Munich announced the emergency hospitalization of its coach Vincent Kompany. The 40-year-old Belgian was attending reserve team training at the Bavarian club’s training center when he felt unwell. Victim of a dislocated shoulder in his fall, the former Manchester City defender ultimately remained away from the field for four weeks due to meningitis. This morning, he made his comeback in front of the media, on the eve of the reception in Cologne, as part of the 10th day of the Bundesliga.

“I am very grateful for the support received. It was an intense period. At the time, it was a unique experience. Looking back, everything worked out perfectly. I immediately received support from the club and the doctors. I am very grateful for that. We try to quickly regain our composure and wait for the results. Everything went well; all tests were positive. I must say that I am in great shape. We have had numerous tests, all of which were conclusive. In this moment of serenity, we also try to learn. I also found it fascinating to observe the team from another angle, to see how the players and staff work. Now I feel great again and I’m happy to be back with the team.”he declared, before explaining that he did not know how to listen to his body just after the C1 shock against the Gunners.

“Against Arsenal, I was transparent about what happened: I was simply sick and maybe I didn’t listen to my body enough. Everything that followed flowed from that. I don’t want to minimize the facts, it was a shock. But it was the consequence of my perseverance when I needed a break. Now I feel good, I’ve been through a long process since then. I hope the situation returns to normal as soon as possible. I am infinitely grateful for the reaction of the club and all the medical staff, who were there day and night. They went out of their way to help me. I would also like to thank my staff, who persevered and allowed us to draw strength during this difficult period. I loved the match against Atlético. It was one of the most memorable moments of my coaching career, even though I wasn’t there. We will remember this memory, and it will give us strength for the future. »

Finally, at a time when current football displays a very busy schedule and ever-increasing pressure, Kompany responded to doubts about his state of health. Is the Belgian sufficiently armed to withstand the shock at the head of a club like Bayern? For the main person concerned, what happened to him is not due to the status of the club he coaches. “I understand the questions that will arise. The truth is that we always tend to overthink when we don’t have all the information. We are undefeated and I took the longest vacation of my life. As a manager, I can’t say there is more pressure than at Burnley in the Championship. It can happen to anyone, and we can learn from it. » The message got through.

MN: Eyes on Manchester – the City and United duo Milan want to bring back to Italy

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

AC Milan are looking for opportunities to bolster the squad ahead of next season, with a particular eye on the north of England.

According to MilanNews, Milan are eyeing Manchester for the January transfer window, but also for next summer. The directors are currently in a phase of monitoring certain situations that could develop later, to their benefit.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Rossoneri are also interested in players whose contracts are expiring within the next 18 months, as was the case with Mario Gila this summer, who signed for the Diavolo one year before his contract with Lazio expires.

Bruno Fernandes is one to watch for the upcoming summer transfer window, with his contract expiring in June 2027 but with an option to extend. United’s attempts to extend his deal have so far been unsuccessful, likely due to the player’s desire to try something else.

Fernandes has played at an incredible level in the Premier League in recent years, winning Player of the Year for 2025-26. So far, he has made 341 official appearances for the Red Devils, scoring 113 goals and providing 111 assists.

He’s a key player for the Portuguese national team and he would provide leadership that the squad could lose if players like Luka Modric leave. Moreover, his past in Italy – where he had spells at Novara, Udinese and Sampdoria – could help him integrate more quickly and effectively.

Fernandes also shares the same agency as Ruben Amorim. The relationship between the two is excellent, and already this summer the Rossoneri had inquired about a possible move for the former Sporting star.

At the other Manchester club, however, there’s an old acquaintance of Italian football who could come back into fashion as early as January: Mateo Kovacic. His contract also expires at the end of the campaign, and he could come in to add depth too.

Given the increasingly certain departure of youngster Christian Comotto, an experienced player like Kovacic – at the right price, in terms of wages – could be useful for Amorim. His situation is being watched, but there will be competition from other clubs, likely in Italy too.

‘Outer Banks’ Alum Madelyn Cline in Final Talks to Join New ‘Serendipity’ Movie (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Madelyn Cline is in final talks to join the new “Serendipity” film in the works at for Amazon MGM Studios and Miramax, Variety has learned exclusively from sources.

The project is described not as a reboot of the 2001 romantic comedy but rather an update in a modern setting featuring new characters. Cline would join previously announced cast members Milo Manheim, Whitney Peak, Stephen Kalyn, and Auliʻi Cravalho. Exact character and plot details are being kept under wraps. Related Stories ‘La Bola Negra’ to Receive International Star Award at Palm Springs International Film Festival (EXCLUSIVE) Brad Pitt Says 'Ocean's 14' Not Shooting Until 'Late' 2027

This would mark the latest major film role for Cline. She is also set to star opposite Glen Powell in the upcoming Judd Apatow comedy “The Comeback King,” which is slated to release in theaters on Feb. 5, 2027. She will also appear in “The Day Drinker” opposite Johnny Depp and Penélope Cruz, with that film hitting theaters March 26. Her past film credits include 2025’s “I Know What You Did Last Summer” and “Glass Onion: A Knives Out Mystery.”

In television, Cline is best known for her role in the hit Netflix drama “Outer Banks,” on which she starred as Sarah Cameron throughout the series’ six-season run at the streamer. She has also appeared in shows like “Stranger Things” and “Vice Principals.”

Cline is repped by CAA, Entertainment 360, Bold Talent Agency, imPRint, and Hirsch Wallerstein Hayum.

Michael Showalter is attached to direct “Serendipity.” Marc Klein, who wrote the original film, will pen the screenplay and executive produce. Showalter is also a producer alongside Jordana Mollick for Semi-Formal Productions. Simon Fields, who produced the original, is also a producer. Jonathan Glickman, Alexandra Loewy, and Thom Zadra will executive produce for Miramax. Arianna D’Andrea is overseeing the project for Miramax, while Emily Crook is overseeing for Semi-Formal.

The original “Serendipity” starred John Cusack and Kate Beckinsale. In the film, their characters meet one night in New York and decide to leave the chance of meeting again up to fate. They ultimately go searching for each other years later despite being in relationships with other people. The film was a box office success, grossing over $77 million on a reported budget of $28 million.

Report Barcelona still eager for Bastoni even with €70m asking price

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There are new reports that Barcelona will schedule meetings for Inter’s Alessandro Bastoni, despite Hansi Flick doubts and the interest growing from Chelsea in the Italy defender.

The centre-back has been on the Blaugrana wish list for several years, and they made a strong push for him over the summer, only to be rejected outright.

His contract only runs to June 2028, and although there are negotiations to extend, they have not reached a conclusion yet and are moving very slowly indeed.

There had been suggestions in recent days that Barcelona had cooled off on Bastoni, because coach Flick was not sure that he would fit well into a four-man defence.

However, Sky Sports Switzerland reporter Sacha Tavolieri and Matteo Moretto on Fabrizio Romano’s Italian language YouTube Channel claim that the Catalans are still eager, planning some new meetings.

The asking price remains €70m, even with the contract running down, and they will discuss the situation between director Deco and coach Flick before deciding whether to dive back in next season.

The most likely time for Bastoni to walk away from San Siro was last summer, when he was still stinging from a series of controversies including his red card in the World Cup play-off against Bosnia and Herzegovina.

Since then, he has won the Scudetto and Coppa Italia Double at Inter, and restored some of his reputation for Italy.

Why Chris Pratt’s Kids Don’t Know What He Does for a Living

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Chris Pratt shared why his kids, whom he shares with wife Katherine Schwarzenegger and ex Anna Faris, don’t know what he does in an exclusive E! News interview at the Way of the Warrior Kid premiere.

Chris Pratt doesn’t want to bring work home with him.

The Parks and Recreation alum recently shared why he has kept his and wife Katherine Schwarzenegger’s kids Lyla, 6, Eloise, 4, and Ford, 2, in the dark when it comes to his career in the spotlight.

“I haven’t quite told my kids what I do for a living so it’s a little weird to show them my movies,” Pratt explained in an exclusive E! News interview at the premiere for his new Apple TV film The Way of the Warrior Kid Nov. 19. “They’ve started watching some movies and this is definitely appropriate for kids of my kids’ ages. I think a 4 or a 6-year-old could definitely watch this movie. However, I might not.”

The 47-year-old—also dad to son Jack, 14, with ex Anna Faris—said that while his oldest is aware of his fame, he hasn’t felt ready to introduce his younger kids to his profession.

“They’ll be like, ‘Dad, why are you on the TV?’” he considered. “We haven’t figured that out yet.”

Of course, he wasn’t ruling out the concept of showing the film, which hits Apple TV Nov. 25, to at least his daughters down the line.

“I’ll have to check with Katherine,” he said, noting that Jack, “has already seen it and he loves it.”

But Pratt emphasizes that his eldest son has certainly proved he can handle any pressure that comes his way.

“Just recently my son Jack was studying for an A Gov test and it was really complicated stuff and they’re getting into some hard academics,” Pratt recalled, “and he was looking at an answer and I said, ‘I don’t know if that’s the right answer or not. That’s kind of like, your teacher’s opinion,’ and he was like, ‘Yeah, but Dad, I want to get an A on this test so if that’s her opinion I want to know that that’s the right answer but I also understand that it might not be the right answer.’”

Nodding toward the nuances of his teen’s thinking, Pratt emphasized, “I was like, ‘Oh this kid is really smart. Like, he’s going places.’”

Meanwhile, Jack’s mom Faris has similarly felt floored by her son’s growth, telling E! News in March that he’s become a “very steady” kid.

“He's kind of a tugboat of a kid,” the 49-year-old—who is also stepmom to her husband Michael Barrett’s two kids—shared. “Emotionally he's not easily rattled, and he's seen a lot, as far as, like, his folks in this industry.”

'Some of it does cross the line' - Omar Berrada hits back at criticism of Man Utd hierarchy ahead of Mauricio Pochettino bow at Liverpool

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United chief executive Omar Berrada has addressed the intense scrutiny currently facing the Old Trafford hierarchy, claiming that some of the personal attacks on club officials have gone too far. As the Red Devils prepare for a high-stakes encounter against Liverpool to mark the beginning of the Mauricio Pochettino era, it appears the board is eager to shift the focus back towards the pitch.

The Red Devils find themselves languishing in 18th place in the Premier League table with just eight points from their opening 10 matches, having managed only one win and five draws during that run. Their dismal start has ignited a firestorm of anger among the global fanbase.

'Being in this position comes naturally with the criticism and scrutiny we're going to get,' Berrada explained during an interview with Sky Sports. 'We accept it. Some of it is fair, some of it does cross the line. We do live in this world of social media, where people like to generate negative stories about the club because it drives more engagement, and it drives more general noise and activity around the club. But we park that.'

The frustration in the stands has not been limited to online platforms. Director of football Jason Wilcox has found himself the specific target of chants and banners at recent fixtures, while calls for both Wilcox and Berrada to depart have gained significant traction across social media networks.

Beyond the executive level, the ownership situation continues to serve as a backdrop for unrest, with supporters regularly chanting for minority owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe to leave the club. Anger towards the Glazer family also remains strong, dating back to their leveraged takeover in 2005, which left Manchester United burdened with debt that has now climbed beyond £1 billion.

The timing of Berrada's comments is significant, coming just days before Pochettino takes charge of his first game as Manchester United head coach. The Argentine faces a baptism of fire with a trip to Anfield to face arch-rivals Liverpool on Sunday.

Pochettino has been appointed as Michael Carrick's successor after the latter managed just one Premier League victory during his spell in charge, a 5-2 win over Ipswich Town on August 30. The fighting spirit shown by United in recent outings was not enough to save Carrick's job, despite the team's remarkable comeback from two goals down to beat Roma 3-2 in the Champions League, followed by another spirited display in their final match before the international break, when they twice fought back from two goals behind to secure a 4-4 draw against Aston Villa.

Pochettino faces a difficult start to life at Old Trafford, with a demanding run of fixtures awaiting him. After Sunday's clash with Liverpool, United will travel to Portugal to face Sporting CP in the Champions League on Wednesday, with the Red Devils sitting 17th in the league-phase table on six points from four matches. They will then host Brentford on November 28 before making another trip, this time to take on Newcastle United on December 2.

Five Plays: North Carolina Fourth Quarter

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

This was the second time that Koen Entringer came off the edge, even though there was motion to his side of the field. This was a deliberate blitz call to sell out to get a sack, and I love it. This is how you force quarterbacks to beat you with their arm and their decision-making. It works on the true freshman here, after it worked against one of the most experienced quarterbacks in the country earlier in the game.

Koen actually does a better job on this blitz with getting flat once he got around the corner. He doesn't go where the quarterback will be, but instead, he comes down the line for a step or two before he breaks down so that he can react to the scramble. If he keeps going full speed, he's going to be left grasping for air. If he angles towards his back shoulder, he's going to risk a broken tackle. He slows down and gets down the line so that he can react to whatever comes his way.

I can't get enough of Keyjuan Brown running the ball as if he only gets to eat dinner if he breaks every tackle on the play. He runs through two guys at the point of attack, spins off his own blocker, then he takes Derek McDonald for a ride six yards down the field.

This is also another well-blocked play by the offensive line. I've been very critical of these guys, but they were really good last weekend. The name of the game is "a hat on a hat," and these guys were very good at meeting that standard. They owned their assignments, which gave Keyjuan a lot of opportunities to get nice gains to move the ball down the field. This was my favorite play, but he had a handful of runs where he picked up 8-10 yards with good space to run.

Lincoln Kienholz had some rough moments in this game, and this was the worst one, in my opinion. With the score and the time in this game, they needed him to get this first down, and I think he just had a genuine moment of uncertainty here. He has the space and angle to get a first down if he commits to running this ball off the read action. Instead, he ends up allowing the safety to split the difference on his option to run or pass. That can't happen with one defender. You force him to commit to one or the other. Kienholz ends up running backwards to make this throw, and then he delivers a poor pass. A really rough moment for him.

I was happy to hear him being hard on himself after the game. It was also good to hear Jeff Brohm look at that as a positive. He'll turn that negative into a driving force to play better going forward.

This is outstanding effort from Tyler Thompson. He explodes off the ball into a bull rush before he loses his footing. He then crawls on the ground to get the sack. No real analysis here. This is just awesome to see.

This was pointed out on the broadcast, but I'll do the same here. I have no clue what Stanquan Clark is doing on this play. He is right over the center to start the play, and he just doesn't really do anything. He takes a few steps back at the snap, and then he just stands still.

I get very excited when this team's best players make the big plays, like we saw Clark make on Saturday. They need the top guys to play to their potential. I tend to get equally frustrated when they have moments like this. It's third and short, and a stop would set up a tough decision to kick the field goal or go for it. Imagine if they could have gotten a stop here late in the game. Instead, Clark is right in the middle of the field on a walk-in touchdown.

The point of this series is to analyse and break down plays, but sometimes it's just nice to marvel at a really good play. Lawayne McCoy makes one of the more underrated plays of the game here by breaking this tackle to get a first down. The Cards needed to run the clock on this drive, and his subtle setup to make this cornerback lean the wrong way on his tackle attempt was so savvy.

Mark Ivey mixed up his pressure looks throughout this game, with safeties coming off the edge with zone behind it, linebackers doing the same with man coverage, and plays like this where Clark comes from depth with a shell zone behind it. They were able to get quick pressure all night long, and you can clearly see how that ups the difficulty level for the quarterback on this play.

Tyler Burgess had looked very good for a true freshman thrust into his first game action, but he can't even get his eyes up to look for a receiver. My view on pass defense is pretty simple. You either cover well enough to force the quarterback to hold the ball and progress through his reads, or you get there quickly enough that they can't make their reads. We see the latter here.

I've watched an absurd amount of football in my lifetime, and I've never seen a game end like this. Especially because if Jordan Shipp possesses this ball for even a split second, it's a huge first down that could lead to a game-winning field goal. Instead, it's a big play for Jabari Mack with a very smart decision to go down to seal the game.
