The Mauricio Pochettino blueprint and one non-negotiable to fix broken Man United

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

With empathetic man-management, a commitment to attacking football and hard-running, and a desire to be involved in recruitment, Pochettino has arrived at Old Trafford with a plan, writes Richard Jolly

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Mauricio Pochettino is the manager Manchester United could, time and again, have appointed in the past. He marked his eventual arrival at Old Trafford by trying to shrug it off; theirs more than his. Pochettino made it to the last two in 2022, when Erik ten Hag was instead chosen, and may have seemed United’s second choice in 2016 and 2019, when Jose Mourinho and Ole Gunnar Solskjaer were preferred. But by pivoting to the future was a way of avoiding the issue of why none of them – nor David Moyes, Louis van Gaal, Ralf Rangnick, Ruben Amorim or Michael Carrick – has truly succeeded.

Pochettino’s plan to fix United may draw on a more distant past, the identity he described that seemed to stem more from Sir Alex Ferguson than his eight immediate successors, but reliant heavily on a positivity about the future. “I don't think too much in the past. I think if we want to create something different and positive and the people feel proud about that, it’s to build our own way.”

His first target, he smiled, was to sleep; unsurprising given that he had a transatlantic night flight after his final game in charge of the United States, in Cincinnati, on Tuesday. A coach who long coveted the United job is relishing a return to club football, away from the stop-start nature of the international game. “I think we are animals of the grass,” he said.

As United are found in the relegation zone, without a league win since August, the temptation is to say his new charges might not be. But Pochettino is seeking to bring empathetic man-management, drawing on his record of making footballers better and fashion change; the hint was that his football will be different from Carrick’s even if a mid-season appointment facing a packed fixture list may have to compromise at first. “Our job is to adapt,” he said.

But it was notable that he said: “We arrived with the idea to create something from scratch, from our own ideas and philosophy, in the way that we love to work. Like always, we need to improve our players.”

And always Pochettino teams have run, normally further and faster than most. Maybe it was unsurprising, then, that he said: “I think the training session was a little bit longer than it normally is in [matchday] minus two.” Yet the United team he inherited have tended to be outrun; only Sunday’s opponents Liverpool have covered less distance in the Premier League this season.

Pochettino argued the statistics do not tell the whole story. “We cannot talk about things without context, and data is about context,” he said. “If you don't put context on the data, I think it's very dangerous to talk and I think it's easy to manipulate the data, but it's true that that sometimes data shows something that you can see on the field. But we are going to be very focused on trying to improve our data, our numbers in every single area of the field.”

Other figures that need an upgrade are the numbers of wins, a mere one, the defensive record, the third worst, the league position of 18th and the numbers that have contributed to United’s worst league start in four decades. If all of that is an indictment of an underachieving squad, Pochettino countered: “The commitment from all the players that we were chatting with was amazing from day one.” Whether all his predecessors would concur with that is another matter, but Pochettino spent three hours in his office on Thursday, talking to them individually. One decision has been made already: he will retain Bruno Fernandes as captain.

“The important thing is to find a way, through our experience, to create that platform for them to express their talent,” Pochettino said. There have been times in the last 13 years when the United players have appeared intimidated rather than liberated. The burden of playing for the club has been great. Pochettino wants to turn the pressure and expectation into a positive. “We need to transform that pressure into energy,” he added.

And he wants them to attack. Carrick bowed out with two games that felt throwbacks, the 3-2 win over Roma and the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa. In the seven goals they scored in five days, Pochettino may have something to build on. “We found a group of players that we proposed to do the football that we feel: being brave, being Manchester United,” he said.

One of the questions of the post-Ferguson era is what being Manchester United really is now. Pochettino has his answer. “I think the culture of the club and the identity of the club is very clear,” he said. “It's an historic club with amazing years competing and being one of the best clubs in the world. It's difficult to find another club the size of club that is Manchester United.”

Certainly there is not one of a similar size in a relegation zone. That is attributable in part to bad recruitment. If hiring Pochettino may change that, a manager with a two-and-a-half year contract wants an input on transfers. “In the future, I would like to be involved in all the main decisions that will happen on the squad,” he said.

That seems crucial in his long-term plan. The short-term part starts at Anfield on Sunday for a manager who is short of sleep but high on optimism.

Mauricio Pochettino confirms Bruno Fernandes role and plans to revamp Man United philosophy

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Pochettino has committed to Fernandes as United’s ‘leader’ as he bids to inspire a high-octane style of football to guide the club out and away from the relegation zone

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Mauricio Pochettino has confirmed Bruno Fernandes will remain his captain at Manchester United as he bids to transform a club in crisis.

Fernandes has come under criticism this season, failing to replicate his from from a historic assist-making season last term.

Yet Pochettino is adamant Fernandes will be United’s “leader” as he bids to inspire a more physical approach to mirror his preferred pressing philosophy ahead of Sunday’s first game against Liverpool at Anfield.

“All the players, we were chatting, it was amazing from day one, Bruno is our captain, an important player,” Pochettino confirmed. “But all the players need to think they are important, from the bottom to the top.

“I spent three hours, chatting in my office, with all the players, maybe I miss some injured players. In the next few days, we will talk in an individual way with all of them. It’s important to listen to what they need to say, understand the place they are. Not only on the field, off the field, what’s going on. Some conversations, some collective way, meetings, but focus is on Sunday.”

While Pochettino added that his priority is to provoke a response after the downturn under Michael Carrick this season, rather than build a long-lasting culture that he insists could take many years to cultivate.

“We arrived with the idea to create something from scratch, our own ideas and philosophy, in the way we like to work, we find supportive people in the club, that make it easy, the adaptation, at the same time, we need to improve the players, to create the way to find a way to perform,” Pochettino said.

“We are not focused on culture, it’s a massive work, you need to spend 10 years to focus on culture, it’s clear, a historic club.

"When we arrive, we found a group of players that we propose to do the football that we feel, being brave, be Man United, you know? It's about proposing the idea, they buy that idea, to be brave and try take that to the field. It's a debate that I don't like to go in, we cannot talk about things without context.

“On the data without context, it's easy to manipulate the data, I need to be honest, sometimes the data can show some things. We'll try improve the data and numbers in every single area of the field. We are here, not to denounce things, we're here to build things, perform and win games.

“I don’t see first choice players, but I pick those who show me more in training. Dovbyk returned from international duty with a different spirit, he has made progress and I am pleased about that.”

Fantasy Football: Jaylen Waddle finally shows up, Bears' backfield goes off and more bold predictions for Week 11

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There aren't many weeks left in the fantasy football regular season; you can't hold anything back. That means you need to be bold to come out on top. Our Yahoo analysts share their one bold prediction for fantasy football in Week 11.

I realize that trusting any WR tied to Bo Nix is like playing with fire. But there have been some fantasy-related improvements to Denver's passing game. First, Nix is throwing more relative to expectations. He's been at a +3.0% PROE over the last month, a 180-degree flip from Weeks 1-4 (-2.5%). He's also throwing to the middle of the field, where passing efficiency jumps, at the sixth-highest rate. And he's fifth in EPA per dropback on those throws.

That's where Jaylen Waddle comes into play. He's led the Broncos in targets on concepts over the MoF (2.49 YPRR). That's where the Raiders have been consistently beaten on defense. Opposing WRs are averaging 87.6 YPG and a smooth 24.8 PPR PPG on those throws alone. While both were on broken plays, both Seahawks' WRs found themselves in the end zone with the ball on throws between the numbers. Assuming Waddle clears protocol, he'll give us his second WR1 performance of the season in Week 11. — Chris Allen

The Bears' backfield will finally be at full strength in Week 11 with Kyle Monangai returning after a five-game absence due to a toe injury. He's picked the right defense to get back on the field against, since the Saints are allowing the second-most fantasy points to RBs, the eighth-most rushing yards and the fourth-most rushing touchdowns. Chicago also has an implied total of 30 points, so we can project a lot of scoring from Ben Johnson's crew.

New Orleans has let 11 different backs reach double-digit fantasy points, including three sets of teammates. One of those duos was Bijan Robinson and Brian Robinson Jr., who both finished as top-12 fantasy RBs in Week 4. Monangai and D'Andre Swift also accomplished that feat in the opener versus the Panthers, which was one of the four games they've shared the field together this season. Get ready for them to do it again, now that the bar is lower with six teams on bye. — Justin Boone

Over the last month, Chig Okonkwo has the seventh-best TE volume and a top-5 route share when accounting for his Week 10 injury. He still has the big-play ability he's had throughout his career and can turn his volume into better production against a Bengals defense ranking bottom-5 versus TEs. Cincinnati is forcing opposing offenses into going pass-heavy, is able to slow down receivers on the outside, but has still allowed the second-most yards on the fourth-most targets to TEs inside, without playing against any of the top fantasy tight ends. If Okonkwo has the ball in his hands that much, he's bound to make some big plays after the catch. — Joel Smyth

I know Cam Ward's name hasn't exactly been synonymous with fantasy scoring, but he's quietly put together two pretty damn good games. Dallas, meanwhile, has been loudly awful on defense for more than two seasons. New defensive play-caller this week? Cool. I'm still calling a top-10 finish for Ward. He's thrown for 535 yards and four touchdowns over his last two games and added another 80 yards on the ground. Dallas is giving up 7.8 yards per pass attempt and has already allowed 19 passing touchdowns. Brock Purdy just hung 389 yards and three scores on them in 28 attempts. Ward doesn't have to do all that. Give me 250 through the air, two touchdown passes and another 35 on the ground. And if Dak Prescott gets Dallas out to an early lead, good. That's more chances for Ward to throw against a defense I'm still going out of my way to attack. — Ray Garvin

The Titans normally don't throw the ball much to Pollard, but that's changed in the last three games — the underrated back has collected 14 catches in that span. And the Dallas defense is especially terrible when it comes to tracking running backs in the passing game — Christian McCaffrey had a field day against this unit last week. Tennessee's offense has been up to code in the last three weeks, and this game features a 54.5-point total. Pollard is going to crush in his revenge game against the Cowboys, and part of the story will be written through the air. — Scott Pianowski

Nottingham Forest manager Oliver Glasner believes Liam Delap could score 20 goals a season if he improves his all-round game.

Forest’s £50m summer signing from Chelsea enjoyed his best day since joining the club with a double to sink Manchester City earlier this month and Glasner believes that game showcased how the 23-year-old can become even more of a threat.

“I think the City game was where he has his biggest strengths, when he has his pace and he can use his power. And, of course, he can finish with right and left,” said Glasner. “But we’re also working with him around his movements in the box where he has a lot of potential with his physicality.

“Sometimes we arrive in the right areas for assists and then he’s not in the right places. This is what he needs to improve.

“I always said he will have double figures regarding goals at the end of the season because this is just how he is. He should keep this and then work on the other parts of football, and then he maybe goes closer to 20 than 10.”

Daniel Farke wants to create another “bit of history” against Chelsea and end Leeds’ 27-year winless run at Stamford Bridge.

No wins and nine defeats in 11 visits to west London, mean Leeds haven’t won at Chelsea since 1999. On snapping that run, Farke said: “Of course for Leeds United, it’s always a bit [of a] special fixture to travel to London to play Chelsea. And we are very respectful, but definitely not fearful.

“We know we have to be at our very best in order to give us a chance to win some points. But this is also why we travel. We want to be competitive.

Overlapping. Underlapping. Inverted. Traditional. The role of full-back has evolved dramatically. Once a defensive-first position that provided some auxiliary attacking support, the role has developed into something far more multifaceted.

Just ask right-back Ben White, who – with Riccardo Calafiori on the left – has been an important figure in Arsenal’s superb start to the campaign. Talking a few years ago, he said: “Playing full-back the way [Mikel Arteta] wants is completely different to any other manager. To play full-back for him, you’ve got to be a centre-midfielder, a centre-back, a winger and a No 10.”

Between them, White and Calafiori have contributed to six goals for Arsenal already in the Premier League this season (White: three assists; Calafiori: one goal, two assists). No other defender has registered more than three goal involvements so far. The Arsenal duo are a great illustration of the impact full-backs can have. But, with the position more demanding than ever before, how does each Premier League team deploy theirs?

Watch Victor Wembanyama's dunk... or 'shrunk' — a jump shot down into the rim

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Victor Wembanyama has 72 dunks this season. To put that in perspective, that not only leads the league (Giannis Antetokounmpo is second with 49), but Wemby has more dunks than 20 NBA TEAMS.

But on Thursday night, Wembanyama did something with a "dunk" we've never seen before.

What is that? A jump shot from zero feet? On the broadcast, Sean Elliott ultimately called it a "shrunk" because it's a shot and a dunk. Officially, it registered as a dunk, but how do you define a dunk? If it's some variation of "the ball is thrown in a downward motion into the basket," then I guess this is a dunk (much like Blake Griffin and others threw the ball down into the rim, although he did it with much more force).

Wembanyama explained after the game he's doing that because he's got a blister on his finger and all the dunks hurt.

Diplo Jokingly Calls Madonna the "Biggest B---h" in Candid Comments

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Diplo playfully described his longtime friend Madonna as being a "b---h her whole life" due to her sleep schedule: "She gets s--t done."

Madonna isn't waiting for Diplo's call night and day, but she'll answer anyway.

After all, the "Lean On" artist revealed that his longtime friend has a rare gene that lets her get by on less sleep. And while that makes her more accessible to chat, her schedule has had some unforeseen side effects.

"I don't need a lot of sleep," Diplo began on the Nov. 18 episode of the Office Of podcast. "People say that all the time. I think I'm lying to myself. I know one person that has this gene, though, is Madonna because she really only sleeps like three hours a night, every night."

When asked if she's "cranky" or a "b---h," Diplo emphasized that the Grammy winner—whom he befriended while working on her 2015 album Rebel Heart—can get anyone down on their knees when she needs something done, jokingly saying the gene is why she's been a "b---h her whole life."

"The biggest b---h," he playfully teased. "But that's her brand. She gets s--t done. She's the one person that will still answer my calls at like 4 in the morning and then again at 7 a.m. if I text her. She's always on the grind."

Though Diplo (born Thomas Wesley Pentz Jr.) isn't hung up on Madonna's mood, he has prayed that he could also work on much less sleep. But he is leaning on some tried-and-true methods to still have a productive day.

"I am really good at taking care of my body. I've learned things that work when you're traveling, like the way I try to exercise when I'm always landing," he explained, "sleeping as much as I can when I can."

"I also wake up with positivity every day," the 48-year-old added. "That matters. I used to not be a morning person. Now, I wake up, I might do a little nicotine pouch or whatever. I wake up and I'm just like, 'What's on the news? What's going on?'"

But envy aside, Diplo—dad to sons Lockett, 16, and Lazer, 12, with ex Kathryn Lockhart, and Pace, 6, with Jevon King, as well as a 15-month-old daughter—has proven he is one of Madonna's biggest fans.

"It already sucks to be a woman in the music industry, but to be a boss woman is even harder,” he told Rolling Stone in 2015. "She sold out her tour in minutes, but no one seems to want her to succeed—'Madonna, we've been there, done that.'"

However, he emphasized, "She created the world we live in."

I guess I should just say it: I'm in love with you,'" she recalled to The New York Times. "We started emailing, and then emailing turned to texting." Yellowjackets Season 4: What clues are hiding in the opening credits?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Yellowjackets is back. With Season 4 comes new mysteries and new opening title cards inviting viewers to speculate on what's next. With each episode, Mashable will be probing these details, looking for questions and their answers. So, buckle up.

The theme song, "No Return" by Craig Wedren and Anna Waronker, roars to life over VHS footage once more. Some images are classic, like pre-crash footage of the team smiling and Jackie (Ella Purnell) smirking on the soccer pitch and drawing a threatening finger across her neck. But others are new.

In Season 4, Episode 1, here are all the curious images we noticed:

Melanie Lynskey is shown wide-mouthed before a microphone, suggesting adult Shauna has something to say (or sing?) in Season 4. Considering Shauna has a special skill for brutal one-liners, whoever she's looking at should be trembling.

Under blood and a prosthetic broken nose, Sophie Nélisse is hard to recognize in the moment. But freeze-frame the credits, and there's no doubt that's Teen Shauna. She's lying in the snow. So, presumably this is still when the girls were in the wilderness.

At the end of the Season 3, Natalie (Sophie Thatcher) successfully reached the outside world through the frog scientist's SAT phone. So, what happens before rescue comes?

The colors of this shot are brighter and more saturated than Yellowjackets tends to go. The uniform is a throwback 1950s style, and actress Tawny Cypress is smiling in a wide-eyed way that's not in keeping with Taissa's character. So, we're speculating this will be another dream sequence or vision. This one feel Lynchian, and as his work touches on doomed romances and the dark underbelly of suburbia, one can assume he'd speak to Taissa on both levels. But what does it mean?

Her face is covered by a mask, but there's no mistaking her frizzy blond hair and glasses. Will the girls' return to civilization show Misty's path to working at the old folks home? Or will this be a dream sequence where she imagines what her future might be?

In the credits, it's a close-up of what appears to be Shauna, wearing a blindfold and holding a string. To get a fuller context, you have to look to the sneak peek images Paramount released on Nov. 5.

Here you can see she's holding a knife on a string while standing inside the crashed plane, her team huddled on the floor around her. This recalls the ritual seance the girls did in Season 1 in the cabin's attic. They'd tried to contact the presumed dead owner of the cabin, and instead tapped into something darker and more mysterious.

The swinging knife is meant to answer the girls' questions. So, are they calling on the "it" of the wilderness for advice ahead of their rescue?

Whose head is being pried open in this nightmarish way? It's hard to tell from the close-up, which gives away no facial features. Dark hair and a sleeve ending in lace are the only clues. But based on the outrageousness, we're going to guess this one is a nightmare within the show.

This set dressing was spotted by fans on Reddit eight months back. But what's its role in the show? Well, we know Jackie's parents have money. Could this be how they memorialize their golden girl?

That's probably bad news for Shauna, right?

Christina Ricci's been a violent ax-wielder before. (We remember Lizzie Borden Took an Ax and The Lizzie Borden Chronicles). We also know an ax is how Edwin died last season — though by Lottie's hand. Yet despite Misty's maniacal facial expression, and her still wearing Natalie's leather jacket, we're guess this is more a Misty-to-the-rescue moment. Maybe she's about to pull a Shining and ax a door?

As this is shot comes from the opening scene of Season 4, there's not much mystery here. Teen Jeff (Jack DePew) had moved on from Shauna and Jackie, getting high with Randy (Riley Baron) and working at Jackie's parents' furniture story. Then he sees her face on the news, with reports of their rescue.

With Shauna finally coming back from the wilderness, we're likely to see how grief and love brought these two together for a marriage we may not envy, but kind of admire?

Yellowjackets Season 4 soundtrack: Every needle drop you need to know

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

As the story of a soccer team gone cannibal kicks off in 1996, Yellowjackets has plumbed the depths of '90s music to make one banger soundtrack season after season. This year, if you're looking to play (or headbang) along, we've got you covered with the needle drops of Season 4 as each episode hits.

But that's not all. Sure, you can use this collection to built a killer playlist. (Buzz, buzz!) We're also going to dive into a bit of what it means and why each song might have been selected for its particular Yellowjackets moment.

Let's kick things off with the Season 4 premiere:

Sample lyric: If I could find that Heina / And that Sancho that she's found / Well, I'd pop a cap in Sancho / And I'd slap her down

Season 4 opens with this hit song that had a total chokehold on teens in 1996. The lyrics sing of a hapless dude making messy decisions.

Fittingly, it plays while teen Jeff (Jack DePew) is getting high on the job at Taylor's Furniture Store with his doofy bestie Randy (Riley Baron). Despite some violent lyrics, "Santeria" is a upbeat tune that speaks to Jeff's laid-back attitude, post-graduation.

But once he sees the news of the Yellowjackets rescue on TV, the theme song cuts in and dramatically changes the episode's tone.

Sample lyric: It was cool / Nothing fire / Nothing broke / Keep simple / Nothing tired / Nothing old / Same as you / Same as you

For four seasons now, this song's cryptic lyrics have played as a pitch-perfect distillation of the show's female rage, rooted in lust, jealousy, and even love.

Sample lyric: My girl, my girl, don't lie to me / Tell me, where did you sleep last night?In the pines, in the pines / Where the sun don't ever shine / I would shiver the whole night through

After the opening credits, Yellowjackets picks up with the titular team in a hospital, where they are safe but look worried, likely about the world learning how it is they made it through their ordeal.

Playing over the sequence is Kurt Cobain's cover of the American folk song also known as "In the Pines," which is recognized as a murder ballad. The lyrics reflect a haunting story of homicide in the woods. The particular song has been covered by a variety of acts since its creation in the 1870s, including the father of Bluegrass Bill Monroe, Blues singer Lead Belly, and Dolly Parton.

Cobain famously covered the song on his MTV Unplugged appearance, on Nov. 18, 1993. So, it's a cover the Yellowjackets themselves might recognize. Moreover, his grunge rendition reflects the trauma and terror of the team's return to civilization.

Sample lyric: For the life of me, I cannot remember / What made us think that we were wise and we'd never compromise / For the life of me, I cannot believe / We'd ever die for these sins, we were merely freshmen

This '90s song plays in the present timeline, but in a meaningful way. Showing up to Shauna's (Melanie Lynskey) door in episode 1, Melissa (Hilary Swank) presents a bottle of wine along with the news that she's moved in next door, and that she's called the press. Shauna looks on in shock asking, "What did you do?" as "The Freshmen" plays.

From 1997, this hit song from the Verve Pipe tells the tale of two young men, reflecting on the damage they've done to a shared lover. In this context, "The Freshmen" plays as if Shauna's subconscious is making an excuse ("we were merely freshmen").

Or perhaps, this song's use use here is meant to reflect Melissa's perspective. As the wounded lover, her story could show what might happen if that lover struck back with the poetic justice of reclaiming her narrative and her future.

Crystal Palace are reportedly interested in re-signing Conor Gallagher, with Thomas Frank keen to strengthen his midfield options ahead of the January transfer window.

The England international has emerged as a potential target for the Eagles, who could bring the former Chelsea midfielder back to Selhurst Park as they look to improve squad quality and depth.

According to a TEAMtalk report, Palace are considering a move for Gallagher as they assess potential additions for the winter transfer window.

The midfielder already understands the demands of playing for Palace and could make an immediate impact without requiring a lengthy adaptation period.

Frank’s interest could give the Eagles an incentive to explore a January move, although convincing Tottenham to sanction his departure could prove challenging.

Fondazione Milan to be celebrated at Frosinone game – the details and Gabbia’s words

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Fondazione Milan will be the Match Charity Partner for AC Milan vs. Frosinone on Sunday, and Matteo Gabbia has spoken as part of the initiative.

Since 2003, the Foundation has used sport as a tool for change, reaching more than 200,000 young people through 288 projects in Italy and 25 other countries. More than €13m has been invested over the years on various projects to help those that are less fortunate.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Before kick-off on Sunday, the beneficiaries of the various schemes will take a photo with the starting lineup. They will also take part in pitch-side activities alongside Massimo Ambrosini, Daniele Massaro and Matteo Gabbia, who recently visited the Calcio Seduto project. Gabbia spoke at the event.

“It’s not easy to sum up in just a few minutes what Franco (Baresi) was like as a person and as a player. He was a key figure for the entire AC Milan club, for us kids who are trying to live up to him, and above all for all those people who always told me about his games.

“It’s always exciting for me to hear people talk about Franco and what he achieved with these colours, as a player and as a man.

“I think Franco is a legend. Fondazione Milan embodies all the beauty and importance that Baresi had. I was moved when I met and got to know the kids from the sitting football team last week.

“I’m sure that if Franco had been there, he would have been proud of everything he organized and created for this foundation.”

A post shared by Fondazione Milan (@fondazionemilan)

“I’m feeling better. It was a strange start to the season, and I was a bit disappointed personally. Hopefully, from the moment I return, it can be a positive season for me too.”

The pregame will also honour the memory of Franco Baresi and his bond with Fondazione Milan. He was a dedicated supporter of its projects, including as a member of its Board of Directors. Club employees will take part in the tribute, along with his longtime teammate and friend Daniele Massaro.

At Sunday’s match, fans can continue to support the Foundation’s projects through donations and auctions of players’ shirts, helping create new opportunities for other young people.

'Crazy' - Stefan Borson reacts to transfer expert's '£500m' Man United January claim

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has "no chance" of splashing £500million on transfers in Janary with the current Manchester United owners.

That is according to former Manchester City financial adviser Stefan Borson, who exclusively told Football Insider that the hierarchy are unlikely to push more funds into the club.

The Glazers and INEOS are under tremendous pressure, even with Michael Carrick sacked to make way for Pochettino, who has only just arrived at Carrington.

Man United's owners are often under the spotlight for their lack of investment on the pitch, with the manager recently escaping a lot of criticism.

Speaking to Football Insider, Bryan King has attempted to provide a sliver of hope, suggesting that the Red Devils could splash upwards of £500m on transfers in January.

Cole Palmer and Lewis Hall are both targets, and have been for quite some time, but any chance of securing their signatures looks very unlikely at this moment in time.

Speaking exclusively to Football Insider, Borson has ruled out the idea of Man United's owners giving Pochettino a war chest in January.

"This Manchester United? There's no chance they're going to do that, is there? Man United have capacity to spend money," he said.

"But it's going to require the owners to put money in, in terms of the cash itself. And in any event, there's literally no chance that United are going to buy Cole Palmer.

"It's pretty unlikely that Newcastle are going to be interested in a deal for one of their best players in January, who's just signed a new contract, for anything less than a crazy price.

"I don't think Man United are going to do it. So I don't really agree with much of that analysis, to be honest."

Man United's owners appear to have settled for mediocrity, and supporters are making their frustrations known from top to bottom.

Instead of investing heavily, they've been focused on reducing the wage bill, and even more funds could soon open up due to exits.

Borson has already told Football Insider that the Red Devils' wage bill simply has to rise again if they are to have any chance of competing at the top level.

The chances of that, however, are very minimal, based on what the owners have done over the last few transfer windows.

Darius Acuff is showing flashes of NBA stardom, but he has one massive problem

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Flip on a Sacramento Kings broadcast and you're likely to be inundated with graphics, statistical queries or fun facts framing Darius Acuff Jr. in an illustrious ensemble.

Names like Michael Jordan, LeBron James, Oscar Robertson and Luka Dončić are frequently mentioned alongside the rookie guard, who is averaging a robust 26.2 points, seven assists (3.9 turnovers) and 3.6 rebounds through the first 14 games of his career. It's an esteemed neighborhood for Acuff to reside in and certainly an encouraging omen as he progresses further into his NBA tenure.

Skeptics, however, will peek beyond the box score and point to Sacramento's 1-13 record, Acuff's relative scoring inefficiency (53.3% true shooting, 24th percentile, per Dunks & Threes) or his ball-dominant tendencies to downplay his raw production. They'll note how much of the Kings' 27th-ranked offense is channeled through him and posit his true impact pales in comparison to those All-Star-level statlines by citing a host of metrics and data.

Stretching back to his sensational freshman season at Arkansas a year ago, Acuff has divided the masses with his play. The first month of his rookie campaign has done little to assuage that polarization, a reality existing independent of anything he's responsible for but rather captures the cantankerous discourse enveloping the sports world more than ever these days.

Nonetheless, there is plenty to examine, praise and critique about Acuff's first 14 games. He's flashed loads of promise and indicated various areas of concern as the Kings presumably build around him moving forward. So, what about his skill-set may suggest future stardom and what may offer reasons for worry in an NBA ecosystem increasingly cruel and unforgiving to smaller guards?

Every star player needs a superpower. Some are more consequential or prominent than others but they all have one. Though not a star at this stage, Acuff's superpower is his ability to maintain a live dribble wherever and whenever he desires. That may sound rudimentary. There are a horde of guards who can haphazardly dribble the air out of the ball. But Acuff makes it immensely practical.

As the league continues to evolve and grow sharper fangs defensively while also upsizing its offensive hubs, dribbling — especially inside the arc or against pressure — is a bit of a lost art. So many players are unable to initiate offense while enduring full-court physicality or slalom through gap help. Acuff can.

He's already one of the most dextrous dribblers in the NBA, equipped to prime screens however necessary, vary the height of his dribble to best suit his intentions at any given moment, wind through treacherous limbs and preserve his handle in the face of point-of-attack pestering. If he wants to avoid stunting defenders or insulate the ball from pressure, he'll keep it low. If it's time to rise for a jumper or make a delivery, he pounds the rock to speed up its arrival into his shooting/passing pocket. The toggling between them is seamless, too.

Acuff is not solely a modern Curly Neal, he also touts an incredibly elastic upper body. Such a trait is evident in his dexterity — he can dribble and pass from a variety of arm and hand placements — but spans beyond it, too.

It empowers him to duck low underneath stiffer defenders or coil around them to find opportunistic angles. The dude gyrates, and it's integral to his offensive signature. Any potential stardom will hinge, in part, on his rare intersection of ball-handling and upper-body flexibility that fuels his playmaking and bucket-getting.

That superpower grants Acuff access to virtually any part of the court. The broader the scope a player can access on the court offensively, the better. It renders him a grander threat to defenses as a scorer and passer. Yet right now, Acuff is not an omnipresent threat. His wondrous ball-handling unlocks doors for him he cannot utilize.

He lacks the burst and vertical explosion to create separation as a driver or aptly elevate as a finisher, issues borne through insufficient lower-body strength that prevent him from generating force downhill or through the air.

He is not an imposing scorer around the rim, consistently reduced to attempts well below the basket or unable to discard help inside, even if momentum is in his corner. His handle and upper-body pliability mitigate some of these troubles but not all of them, which rear their head enough to disrupt his game and narrow the avenues where he's a daunting offensive conductor.

According to Cleaning the Glass, 17% of his field goal attempts occur at the rim (30th percentile among point guards) while he's converting just 58% of those attempts (35th percentile). Similarly, he's currently unable to overwhelm defenders and reliably draw fouls. Despite touting the league's sixth-highest usage rate (34.7%) and having taken the third-most shots, he's 39th in free throws attempted (68).

Just look how groundbound he is on these drives, missing both the pop to ease his finishes or the ferocity to challenge bigs inside:

A lean diet of shots at the rim and charity stripe makes for a tough life in building an efficient scoring repertoire. Until or if Acuff adds significant lower-body strength (and gradual increments could facilitate this, it's not all or nothing), the path toward efficient high-volume scorer is to embrace the 3-ball.

He's already doing so off the bounce, launching the league's third-most pull-up triples per game at 6.9. Although he's only netting 32% of those, it's clearly a shot he's hunting and cozying up with, largely against drop coverage around Domantas Sabonis' bludgeoning screens. But he'll also dance into them over switches and still hoist when a trailing point-of-attack defender is re-entering the play.

Thirty-two percent cannot suffice long-term but it's far from a disastrous launching pad. As he approaches his prime, it's entirely plausible the newly minted 20-year-old levels up as a shotmaker to better suit the volume with which he's evidently found comfort.

If Acuff ascends to a requisite level as a long-range pull-up shooter that warps defenses into dissuading those looks, it should benefit his interior pursuits. Deep drop coverage is an arduous tactic for him to attack inside because of aforementioned physical limitations. But seeing more bigs switch or play around the level — and away from the paint — could allow him to weaponize his handle and flexibility into drives without the fear of a rim protector lurking.

Ideally, the Kings will also add another bona fide creator alongside Acuff, whose off-ball game has yet to be spotlighted much because of the rickety ball-handling carousel surrounding him. Among starting guards, his 49.8% on-ball rate ranks third behind Jalen Brunson and Andrew Nembhard, the latter of whom has played much of the year without Tyrese Haliburton (per databallr).

In the rare moments he's been afforded to operate off the ball, his instincts locating space, traversing screens or curling into dribble handoffs lend credence to the notion his sprawling ball dominance is a product of Sacramento's cumbersome infrastructure more than his individual nature.

Even in the small sample he's shared the floor with Malik Monk — who's struggled mightily this year but is still the Kings' most viable non-Acuff creator as someone who can dribble, initiate possessions and operate a ball-screen — Acuff's efficiency spikes without suppressing his volume. Whether it's spotting up, being deployed as a screener or piercing a tilted defense, he's yielded the rewards of defenses fixating on someone else.

Through 14 games, Acuff is shooting 41.7% (15-of-36) on catch-and-shoot threes. An environment promoting more of those chances and opportunities for his quick-witted, malleable decision-making — Acuff adapts his shooting and passing processes on the fly quite well — would simplify his responsibilities without dimming his light.

That doesn't mean Acuff is ill-suited for an on-ball role, he's exhibited reasons to believe in him there. But the extent to which Sacramento relies upon him for it seems detrimental to himself and the collective offense.

Between occasional minutes next to Monk and his screening/handoff camaraderie with Sabonis, the Kings do present a few helping hands for their rookie point guard. That all evaporates defensively, though. There's hardly any safety net.

They're a horrendous rim protection team; opponents shoot a league-high 75.6% at the basket against them, per Cleaning the Glass. They don't have established point-of-attack stoppers or slippery screen navigators. They switch the majority of actions 1-4 because most of their rotation players cannot fight around picks and that makes Acuff a moving target for opposing offenses.

Despite a litany of debilitating factors, Acuff does himself no favors either. He is, thus far, a very, very poor defender. He applies scant physicality on the ball, gets trapped in screens like a never-ending maze, hardly ever forces turnovers or grabs rebounds and is often ill-positioned off the ball without the tools to recover punctually.

Habits like these gleaned from the eye test are reinforced by various datapoints. Per Cleaning the Glass, with Acuff on the floor, opponents take 6.9% more of their shots at the rim, log a 2.1% higher offensive rebounding rate and turn the ball over 3.6% less of the time. His STOP% (steals, blocks recovered, offensive fouls drawn per 100 possessions) is 1.6 and ranks in the 6th percentile, according to databallr.

Playing for the league's 30th-ranked defense exacerbates the magnitude of Acuff's woes but he is culpable for the vast majority of his shortcomings and must improve in the coming years. He does not make offenses feel his presence and appears to simply drift through possessions instead of committing to sustainable defensive efforts.

As defensive-minded players blossom into loftier offensive duties, we sometimes see them regress defensively. Maybe, through an optimistic lens, the opposite can unfold for Acuff. He shoulders a titanic offensive load and has displayed the capacity to function off the ball in a lower-stress role.

For that to ignite meaningful defensive growth, though, Acuff probably has to infuse substantial lower-body strength. It'll make him sturdier and more liable to anchor against contact, which he's mostly shied away from altogether, and accelerate his lateral mobility, where he concedes breakdowns because he cannot slide quickly enough to cut off ball-handlers.

Achieving this could address glaring limitations on both sides of the ball and amplify his superpowers — the very ones propelling him into graphics of glory alongside Hall of Famers. Maybe someday, Acuff, too, will be the sort of complimentary company rookie guards hope to join if they burst onto the NBA scene.

ตอบทุกคำถาม แทงบอลเว็บไหนดี ในปี 2026

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

สำหรับสายเชียร์บอลที่กำลังลังเลว่าปี 2026 นี้จะ แทงบอลเว็บไหนดี บอกเลยว่าเลือกผิดชีวิตเปลี่ยนแน่นอน ยุคนี้เว็บบอลมีให้เลือกเพียบ แต่จะหาเว็บที่ใช่ ปลอดภัย 100% แถมจ่ายจริงไม่จกตา ไม่ใช่เรื่องง่าย เพราะนอกจากต้องดูว่าเป็นเว็บตรงไม่ผ่านเอเย่นต์ไหมแล้ว ยังต้องเช็กทั้งค่าน้ำ ระบบฝากถอนออโต้ และความชัวร์เรื่องการเงินอีก บทความนี้เลยขอรวบรวมทุกคำตอบ เจาะลึกจุดเด่นแบบเน้นๆ พร้อมวิธีเลือกเว็บบอลตัวท็อปปี 2026 ให้คุณเอาไปใช้ตัดสินใจกันได้แบบง่ายๆ แทงสนุก แถมทำเงินได้จริงแน่นอน

สำหรับสายเชียร์บอลที่สงสัยว่าปี 2026 นี้จะเลือก แทงบอลเว็บไหนดี การพิถีพิถันคัดสรรแพลตฟอร์มก่อนสมัครแทงบอล ถือเป็นเรื่องสำคัญมาก เพราะยุคนี้การให้บริการแทงบอลออนไลน์ มีหลากหลายรูปแบบ การเลือกเว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ไม่เพียงช่วยเซฟเงินในกระเป๋า แต่ยังทำให้ลงเดิมพันได้อย่างสบายใจในระยะยาว ด้วยปัจจัยหลักๆ ดังนี้

หากคุณกำลังมองหา เว็บแทงบอลเว็บไหนดี ในปี 2026 การเลือกเว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ถือเป็นหัวใจสำคัญ การันตีด้วยความเป็นเว็บแทงบอล เชื่อถือได้ การเงินมั่นคง ค่าน้ำดี ค่าคอมสูง ฝากถอน True Wallet ไว ใช้งานง่ายผ่านแทงบอลออนไลน์ผ่านมือถือ และมีทีมงานดูแล 24 ชั่วโมง เพื่อให้คุณสมัครเว็บบอล แล้วเล่นได้เงินจริง 100% นี่คือ 8 อันดับแพลตฟอร์มยอดนิยมที่ตอบโจทย์คำถาม แทงบอลเว็บไหนดีสุด ในปีนี้

UFA248 เว็บแทงบอลอันดับ1 ที่ขึ้นชื่อว่าเป็นเว็บแทงบอลที่ดีที่สุด ส่งจากบริษัทแม่ UFABET ที่ให้ค่าน้ำ 4 ตังค์ คืนค่าคอมสูง 0.5% การเงินมั่นคง เป็นเว็บพนันบอล ไม่ผ่านเอเย่นต์ ที่รองรับฝากถอนวอเลทและธนาคารใน 3 วินาที ใช้งานผ่านมือถือลื่นไหล มีแอดมินดูแล 24 ชั่วโมง สมัครแทงบอลง่าย เล่นได้จ่ายจริง 100%

UFAPLUS เว็บบอล ยุคใหม่รวมกีฬาออนไลน์ และคาสิโนออนไลน์ ครบครัน ค่าน้ำดี ค่าคอมสูง การเงินชัวร์ 100% เป็นเว็บพนันบอลออนไลน์ ที่รองรับฝากถอนวอเลท ใช้งานง่ายทุกระบบปฏิบัติการ พร้อมทีมงานดูแลระดับ VIP ตลอด 24 ชั่วโมง เล่นได้จ่ายจริงแน่นอน

UFA246 แพลตฟอร์มเว็บไซต์แทงบอลเว็บตรงยูฟ่าเบท ปลอดภัย 100% ครบทั้ง แทงบอลเต็ง, แทงบอลสเต็ป, แทงบอลสูงต่ำ และแทงบอลสด ค่าน้ำคุ้มค่า คืนค่าคอมสูง ฝากถอนออโต้ผ่าน True Wallet สะดวกสบาย จัดเป็นเว็บแทงบอลออนไลน์ ที่รองรับมือถือทุกระบบ พร้อมบริการ 24 ชั่วโมง

UFA289 เว็บแทงบอล UFABET ชั้นนำที่สายการเงินมั่นคงไว้วางใจ ค่าน้ำดิบดีที่สุด คืนค่าคอมสูงทุกสัปดาห์ การันตีจากกระทู้เว็บพนันบอล ดีที่สุด pantip ฝากถอนออโต้รองรับวอเลท รวดเร็ว ปลอดภัย เข้าเล่นแทงบอลออนไลน์ ได้ทุกอุปกรณ์ พร้อมดูแลตลอด 24 ชั่วโมง

SAWAN168 เว็บบอลออนไลน์ มาแรงสำหรับนักพนันบอล ได้มาตรฐานเว็บพนันบอล ถูกกฎหมาย อัปเดตราคาน้ำ Real-time ให้ค่าคอมสูง ถอนไม่อั้น ฝากถอนวอเลทไม่มีขั้นต่ำ หน้า เว็บไซต์แทงบอล เสถียรบนสมาร์ตโฟน มีทีมงานบริการ 24 ชั่วโมง ตอบโจทย์คนที่ลังเลว่า สมัครแทงบอล เว็บไหนดี

KO888 เว็บพนันบอลที่ดีที่สุด ที่โดดเด่นเรื่องค่าน้ำดีและค่าคอมสูง เป็นเว็บพนันที่คนเล่นเยอะที่สุด เหมาะทั้งมือใหม่ที่กำลังศึกษาวิธี แทงบอลยังไง และเซียนบอลอาชีพ ฝากถอนออโต้ True Wallet ทันใจ รองรับแทงบอลออนไลน์ ผ่านมือถือ มีแอดมินสแตนด์บายดูแล 24 ชั่วโมง

SAWAN246 ศูนย์รวมพนันบอล ครบวงจร ค่าน้ำแคบช่วยให้แทงบอลวันนี้ ทำกำไรได้ง่ายขึ้น พร้อมค่าคอมสูง ถอนเงินไวผ่านวอเลท ปลอดภัย จ่ายจริง 100% ตอบโจทย์คนค้นหาแทงบอลออนไลน์เว็บไหนดี ได้อย่างตรงจุด เป็นเว็บพนันบอลดีที่สุด พร้อมบริการ 24 ชั่วโมง

KO789 เว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ปลอดภัย จ่ายจริงทุกยอด อัตราค่าน้ำดี ค่าคอมสูง มีวิธีแทงบอลในเว็บ ที่เข้าใจง่าย ฝากถอนผ่านวอเลทแม่นยำ รองรับทุกอุปกรณ์ทั้งมือถือและคอมพิวเตอร์ เข้าเล่นได้ตลอดเวลา พร้อมทีมงานดูแล 24 ชั่วโมง

หากคุณกำลังตามหาคำตอบว่า แทงบอลเว็บไหนดี ที่เล่นแล้วปลอดภัย ไม่โดนโกง การเลือกแพลตฟอร์มที่เป็น เว็บพนันบอล ไม่ผ่านเอเย่นต์ คือหัวใจสำคัญที่สุดในการเล่น แทงบอลออนไลน์ ยุคนี้ นี่คือ 5 วิธีเช็กแบบเจาะลึกที่สายเชียร์บอลใช้คัดกรอง เว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด เพื่อให้คุณสมัครเว็บบอล ได้อย่างมั่นใจ

เมื่อต้องตัดสินใจว่า เว็บแทงบอลไหนดีสุด ในปี 2026 สิ่งสำคัญที่มองข้ามไม่ได้เลยคือ “ราคาน้ำ” (Odds) เพราะราคาน้ำคือตัวกำหนดผลกำไรและช่วยเซฟเงินทุนของคุณโดยตรง การเลือกเว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ที่เปิดราคาบอล 4 ตังค์ คืนค่าคอมมิชชั่นสูง จะช่วยให้ทำกำไรระยะยาวจากการแทงบอลออนไลน์ ได้คุ้มค่าที่สุด หากคุณสมัครแทงบอล บนเว็บแทงบอล เชื่อถือได้ มักจะมีตัวเลือกประเภทค่าน้ำให้ปรับใช้ตามสไตล์การเดิมพัน ซึ่งแต่ละแบบมีความแตกต่างกันดังนี้

สำหรับมือใหม่ที่ยังลังเลว่า แทงบอลออนไลน์ เว็บไหนดี ให้ทำเงินได้ไวและเสี่ยงน้อยที่สุด หัวใจสำคัญคือการเลือกรูปแบบการเดิมพันบนเว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ให้เหมาะกับทุนและเทคนิคของตัวเอง เพราะบนเว็บแทงบอลออนไลน์ ยุคนี้ไม่ได้มีแค่การทายผลแพ้ชนะทั่วไป การเข้าใจ 5 รูปแบบยอดฮิตก่อนสมัครแทงบอล จะช่วยให้คุณวางแผนทำกำไรได้แม่นยำยิ่งขึ้น

การเลือกเว็บแทงบอล ออนไลน์ เว็บไหนดี ที่รองรับระบบ ฝาก-ถอน Auto และ True Money Wallet ควรพิจารณาเว็บตรงที่มีความน่าเชื่อถือ ได้มาตรฐานสากล และปรับอัตราค่าน้ำคงที่

การคัดเลือกโปรโมชั่นคุ้มค่า ถือเป็นตัวช่วยเพิ่มทุนชั้นดี แต่หากเลือกโดยไม่เช็กเงื่อนไขให้ถี่ถ้วน เครดิตฟรีที่ได้มาอาจกลายเป็นภาระในการถอนเงิน การพิจารณา เว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด จึงต้องดูควบคู่กับความยากง่ายของเงื่อนไขการทำยอดหมุนเวียน (Turnover) ด้วย

ยอดเดิมพันสะสมรวมทั้งหมด (ได้+เสีย) ที่ระบบกำหนดให้คุณต้องเล่นให้ครบตามเกณฑ์ก่อนถอนเงินออก

สูตรคำนวณ: (เงินฝาก + โบนัส) x จำนวนเท่าเทิร์น = ยอดเดิมพันสะสมที่ต้องทำ

สำหรับนักเดิมพันยุคใหม่ที่เน้นความสะดวกและต้องการแทงบอลออนไลน์ ผ่านมือถือ คำถามสำคัญคือจะเลือก แทงบอลเว็บไหนดี ที่หน้าเว็บไม่ค้าง และไม่ต้องเสียเวลาตามหา วิธีแทงบอลในเว็บ หรือค้นหาทางเข้าใหม่ทุกครั้งที่ลิงก์โดนบล็อก การเลือกเว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด ที่รองรับแอปพลิเคชันโดยตรง จะช่วยแก้ปัญหานี้ได้อย่างเด็ดขาด

การวิเคราะห์บอลกับการเลือกเว็บบอร์ดหรือ เว็บแทงบอลออนไลน์ เป็นสองปัจจัยที่ต้องทำงานควบคู่กัน หากวิเคราะห์แม่นยำแต่เลือกเว็บที่ไม่มีประสิทธิภาพหรืออัตราราคาค่าน้ำเสียเปรียบ โอกาสทำกำไรระยะยาวก็จะลดลงอย่างมาก

การคัดเลือกแทงบอลเว็บไหนดี ในปี 2026 เพื่อให้ได้เว็บแทงบอลดีที่สุด เชื่อถือได้ หัวใจสำคัญที่สุดคือการเดิมพันกับเว็บตรงไม่ผ่านเอเย่นต์ การันตีความปลอดภัย 100% การเลือกเว็บพนันบอล ดีที่สุด จะช่วยให้คุณสมัครเว็บบอล และวางเดิมพันได้อย่างมั่นใจ ถอนเงินได้จริงทุกยอดบิล

หากคุณยังสงสัยว่า สมัครแทงบอล เว็บไหนดี การเลือกระหว่าง 8 แบรนด์ชั้นนำ (UFA248, UFA246, SAWAN168, UFA289, UFAPLUS, KO888, SAWAN246, KO789) ตามคำแนะนำจากผู้เล่นจริงในกระทู้ เว็บพนันบอล ดีที่สุด Pantip, Google, Facebook หรือ Tiktok จะเป็นทางเลือกที่ปลอดภัย คุ้มค่า และตอบโจทย์การทำกำไรได้จริงชัวร์ 100%

‘Josephine’ Will Floor You

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

This is the question that an eight-year-old named Josephine (Mason Reeves) asks her dad, Damien (Channing Tatum). She’s a sporty kid, the kind of youngster who accompanies her jockish pops on long runs through Golden Gate Park every Sunday morning. It’s during one of these weekly father-daughter bonding sessions that Josephine sprints ahead of her dad and strays off their usual path. Stopping to catch her breath, she sees a female jogger go into a public restroom at the bottom of a hill. Then Josephine watches as a stranger pulls the woman out, knocks her to the ground, and sexually assaults her.

The cops arrest the perpetrator, the survivor goes to the hospital, and eventually, Damien is left with his daughter and that question, hanging in the air. Josephine can’t comprehend what she’s witnessed. Her father doesn’t know how to answer. Instead of comforting or explaining, Damien just resumes their usual training exercises; he’s got to strengthen her up for soccer, he tells his daughter. His intentions are noble. He wants to protect her by pretending nothing’s happened. But Josephine’s father is simply the first adult who fails to help her process the traumatic experience this young girl has gone through. He won’t be the last.

The Grand Jury Prize winner at this year’s Sundance Film Festival, Josephine doesn’t just follow its namesake as she’s forced to navigate a sudden loss of innocence; it sticks so closely to her perspective that you’re allowed to see every confusing gesture, every mixed-message reply, every argument among her parents, and every acting-out response through her eyes. That itself isn’t unusual — most coming-of-age movies let you walk miles in their hero’s Converse All Stars. Yet writer-director Beth de Araújo’s sophomore film handles this shattering take on a kid wrapping her head around trauma with such grace, such delicacy, such force and a lack of false notes, that the work feels seismic even in its silences. It’s not interested in debating how crimes are handled by the cops or the courts, or how society talks around instead of about sexual violence in a constructive way. The movie wants you to reckon with the impact of someone figuring out a foreign landscape filled with unforeseen terrors. Josephine, c’est moi. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

So much of this rests on the tiny shoulders of Reeves, a first-time actor who seems to intuitively understand how to simply be in front of the camera. When Araújo isn’t replicating Josephine’s point of view, letting things unfold from a lower angle looking up or glimpsed from a distance, she’s training her lens directly on Reeves’ face in close-up. You can see the character attempting to grasp and contextualize situations for which she has no reference point in real time. And when Josephine begins to parrot statements and repeat patterns of behavior — she sees Damien spit on the rapist, then later spits on her dad in a fit of rage — we can see her trying on toxic versions of adulthood for size. It’s an incredible performance by any parameter, much less one delivered by a preteen.

Josephine makes space for her parents as well, with Tatum weaponizing his varsity-athlete physicality and kinder, gentler masculinity to play Damien as a well-meaning yet grasping dad, a paterfamilias in over his head when it comes to talking about tough subjects. His solutions: Take a self-defense class and either sweat out the pain or bury it. Gemma Chan plays Josephine’s mom, a dancer named Claire, as someone both more sympathetic to their child’s bewilderment and equally adrift. (A sequence in which she’s asked by her daughter if she’s ever been assaulted is all the more painful by the lack of an actual answer; Chan’s look alone tells you more than you feel you should know.) Tempers get lost, voices get raised, and Josephine herself lashes out in ways that suggest she doesn’t know how to deal with the emotional turbulence coursing through her. There’s a scene that takes place in a partially painted nursery that you may require you to cover your eyes. Trending Stories Marlon Jackson Reveals Stage 4 Prostate Cancer Diagnosis Selena Gomez Shares Her Reaction to Martin Short Ruining Her Wedding Cake Some Gamers Are Pissed That ‘GTA VI’ Makes Them Be Nice to Their Girlfriend Pearl Jam Plan ‘Big-Boy Conversation’ About New Drummer Related Content How a 1972 Cocktail Party Gave Us the Best Documentary of the Year Channing Tatum Says He Hasn’t Been Harassing Zoë Kravitz and Harry Styles With Burner Account ‘The Weight’ Isn’t a Perfect Homage to 1970s Tough-Guy Movies. But It’s Close ‘I Want Your Sex’ Works Out the Kinks of a BDSM Relationship

You don’t need to know that Josephine comes from an extremely personal place to feel that Araújo (whose debut, a Neo-Nazi Karen parable titled Soft and Quiet, now feels like an artist clearing her throat before speaking harshly and loudly) is unpacking this youngster’s story with an extraordinary amount of acuity and sensitivity, even as she refuses to pull punches: The assault is shown forensically, pitilessly, and in full. So is Josephine’s cross-examination on the stand by the defense’s lawyer (Dana Millican) when the child is forced to testify as a witness, which represents the ways our legal system can put everyone involved on trial. Surreal touches, such as the ghostly presence of the rapist (Philip Ettinger) hovering around everyday situations, add to the feeling that you’re viewing all of this through a highly subjective lens. Josephine sees this person everywhere, as a phantom both menacing and eerily childlike. So why shouldn’t we?

“Do you think the man is bad on the inside?” Josephine asks one of her caretakers. “Or good on the inside, but does bad things?” Nobody really knows the answer to that, she’s told. Neither Araújo nor the movie itself pretends to have a satisfying reply. But it’s desperate to ask the question and push you to wonder how a child, in some ways wise beyond her years yet still very much a kid, can reckon with a morality that exists in deepening shades of gray. Josephine is a movie that begins in a moment of fear — Josephine is scared to run out of her garage before the automatic door closes — and ends with a portrait of courage under fire. That the courage comes from someone who can barely comprehend her own bravery only makes the moment that much more impressive. It’s hard to think of a work of art that feels this simultaneously scarring and healing. Or that is tougher to watch yet something that must be seen as soon as possible and by any means necessary.

Conte on Chelsea success story, meeting Kante and Fabregas destiny

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Antonio Conte reveals what changed to win the Premier League at Chelsea, the Arsenal player that ‘killed’ them, his funny reaction to meeting N’Golo Kante, and why Cesc Fabregas was ‘destined’ to be a coach.

The coach is currently waiting for his next role, after walking away from Napoli with the Scudetto and Supercoppa Italiana in his two seasons at the Stadio Diego Armando Maradona.

He sat down with the Cronache di Spogliatoio podcast and discussed his long career as a coach, including the experience at Chelsea from 2016 to 2018.

In that spell at Stamford Bridge, he won the Premier League title immediately, then the FA Cup, but admits it required a radical rethink of his Juventus tactics.

“I had this sort of 3-3-4 formation at Juventus, so when I went to Chelsea, I started with the idea of a 4-2-4 formation, but we didn’t have the players at that moment to make it work,” said Conte.

“I had Diego Costa, Eden Hazard, Wilian and Pedro as the wide men, and Oscar as a second striker, so I used a 4-2-3-1 with all of them and kept some of the same movements.

“We had started well, but after every game I told the staff, we are winning, but I don’t feel this is my team. We were top of the table, but after the break for international duty we took on Francesco Guidolin’s Swansea and it was chaos.

“Anything could’ve happened, it was 2-2, but when there is a sense of instability, these are not my teams. I need sides who control the game. I felt that I didn’t have complete control.”

The turning point came from a painful defeat to local rivals Arsenal.

“It was my first season in the Premier League, but there were Pep Guardiola, Jurgen Klopp, Jose Mourinho, all the best managers. We lost 2-1 at home to Liverpool, then were 3-0 down at half-time to Arsenal.

“Hector Bellerin was killing us down the left, it was one of the few times I was ashamed,” said Conte on the Italian language podcast.

“I realised that was the time I had to change, because I needed to give more protection to Oscar.

“I put Ivanovic on the right with David Luiz and Cahill, Azpilicueta on the right, basically a 3-4-3. It was the turning point, as the next game against Hull City I put Azpilicueta in the back three, he is a soldier and works wherever you put him, and Moses who Chelsea had wanted to sell.”

Conte also revealed that some of the meetings with Chelsea players didn’t quite go as planned, but they worked out in the end.

“It was not a transfer session worthy of Chelsea. Kante had won the Premier League title with Leicester City, I asked for two players, Wanyama and Kante. I’d seen Kante play several times, with him it was as if we were playing with 12 or 13, he never felt tired.

“He walked in behind his agent, I couldn’t even see him. Kante is not a tall man, but he looked like a child, with this big smile. I thought, I expected him to be taller…

“I hoped I hadn’t made a mistake asking for this player, the Premier League they were all huge and physically strong, but the fear only lasted a minute. I spoke to him, Kante is one of the best players I ever coached, and an exceptional lad.”

Conte reveals the aim at Chelsea that season was for a top 10 finish, but they ended up winning the Premier League.

“We won 13 games in a row, that is when I felt this was my team, it was in control. Before that, playing three at the back was practically blasphemous in England, but now it has become a cult and many teams do it.”

Cesc Fabregas was in that Chelsea team too, and has now gone on to become a coach in his own right at Como, qualifying for the Champions League.

“His curiosity, his desire to learn, to modify, Cesc was destined for greatness,” insisted Conte.

“He was a thinker on the field too, so that helped. He could see the move before he got the ball, was in the right position to receive it. When you get these players who think, they are destined to be coaches.”

Man United predicted lineup vs. Liverpool: Mauricio Pochettino makes Lisandro Martinez decision

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Ben Knapton | 20 Nov 2026 16:39

Manchester United manager Mauricio Pochettino could recall Lisandro Martinez to his starting lineup for his opening game in charge away to Liverpool on Sunday in the Premier League.

The Argentine defender is available to his compatriot after completing a domestic suspension and could now slot back into the rearguard alongside Harry Maguire, who has seemingly shaken off a back problem.

Noussair Mazraoui would be the most likely to make way if Martinez returns, as Leny Yoro shuffles over to right-back, on the opposite side to the much-maligned Diogo Dalot.

The Portugal international's withdrawal was cheered by fans in the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa, but with Patrick Dorgu operating in a more attack-minded role of late and Luke Shaw still doubtful, Dalot should reprise his role.

The same goes for Youri Tielemans and Kobbie Mainoo in the middle, as Pochettino sticks to a 4-2-3-1 setup for his maiden match and forces Andrey Santos to wait his turn.

The front four of Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes, Matheus Cunha and Bryan Mbeumo is likely to remain the same too, although Marcus Rashford (hip) is on the verge of returning and could be an option on Merseyside.

Carlos Baleba (hamstring), Manuel Ugarte (knee) and Benjamin Sesko (calf) all remain out of contention for the away side.

Manchester United possible starting lineup:

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Dalot; Tielemans, Mainoo; Diallo, Fernandes, Cunha; Mbeumo

Here Are The Best Saves To Start In FM 27

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you're on the hunt for an exciting new challenge in FM 27, here are the teams we think you should manage (and why!).

If you're on the hunt for a new Football Manager 27 save to either push the limits of your ability or just be a fun romp to trophy after trophy, then we have a list of teams to consider.

These saves mix challenging relegation battlers with high-expectation teams, each with its own challenges. This is different from challenge saves, but each team comes with a few unique goals you'll need to achieve in order to be considered a success at the club.

Coventry City fell out of the Premier League in the 00/01 season and spent the next 25 years slowly declining to League 2, before an incredible run under manager Mark Robins brought them to within one penalty kick of returning to the Premier League. Robins would ultimately lose his job and be replaced by Chelsea and England legend Frank Lampard, who, in his first full season with the team, would win the Championship and return the Sky Blues to the promised land.

Your job is clear in FM 27. Save Coventry City from relegation, then attempt to build on that achievement to take them to European glory. Coventry City spent a lot of money on players in the off-season, so you don't have much of a budget to work with, and the squad is a huge mix of players. Young, raw talent like Caleb Yirenkyi and Sidiki Cherif, and experienced PL heads like Ethan Pinnock and Taiwo Awoniyi, are supported by unproven top-division talent like Bobby Thomas, Jack Rudoni, Milan van Ewijk, Carl Rushworth, and Tatsuhiro Sakamoto.

It's your job to find your best 11 as soon as possible and get into a good rhythm to scrape survival. However, the season kicks off with a brutal schedule, playing Arsenal, Man City, and Brighton in their first five games.

It's been a long, hard road back to success for Sports Interactive, but FM 27 is the game I hoped it would be and so much more.

We're heading over to the German leagues next, with Wolfsburg under heavy duress after relegation and under huge financial pressure. Following relegation from the Bundesliga for the first time in its history, Wolfsburg now has to fight back in a competitive 2. Bundesliga, or risk its financial problems swallowing the club into further dismay.

You're going to rely on a small squad and look to bring in cheap but talented players, or high-potential youth, to navigate the long season. Expectations are high despite the issues surrounding the club, so see if you're up to the task by taking over this former Bundesliga champion.

One of the most unique stories in football history, Portuguese second-division side Torreense actually won the Taça de Portugal last season, earning a spot in the Europa League while not being a top-division side. This means you'll need to balance European participation with trying to go as far as you can, while also aiming for promotion to Portugal's top division.

European participation is a hugely lucrative bonus, and it could fund the club for a few years if you manage to earn some wins. However, expectations are high for the league, so the last thing you want to do is put promotion at risk for the chance to participate in the Europa League or Conference League.

This is a hugely fun save to tackle, and it's interesting to see the player interest be quite a bit better for a second division club than you might expect, all thanks to the pull of European football.

Following a historically bad Championship campaign last year, and financial issues that would have killed most clubs in the same situations, Sheffield Wednesday find themselves in League One, and with a squad that isn't necessarily going to walk the league.

That's where you come in: this club is simply too big to keep slumming it in the third tier, so you need to return them to the Championship, and then ultimately to the Premier League, as quickly as possible.

You'd be relying almost entirely on club reputation to attract players, because the purse strings are tight, and the last thing you need is further points deductions ot undo any hard work.

Dutch Eredivisie side AZ Alkmaar are a bit of a wonderkid factory in FM 27, with at least one hugely talented player in five positions (Bendeguz Kovacs, Quenten Attigah, Andrea Natali, Kees Smit, Elijah Dijkstra). It's your job to raise them to their full potential and start a new era in Dutch football, taking over from the likes of Ajax, Feyenoord, and PSV Eindhoven.

Or, of course, you can maximize profits by selling these wonderkids to fund more established players and make a title push instead. The route you take is up to you, but one thing is certain: AZ are a very fun club to manage in FM 27.

A club with a growing reputation thanks to Manager/Owner Marc White, Dorking recently reached heights in the National League, but suffered relegation and found themselves back in the National League South, with a strong squad.

It's expected that you'll gain promotion back to the National League, but the expectations don't stop there: if you are successful, you'll be asked to reach the playoffs in your first season back.

The new Tactical Effectiveness feature makes managing teams in lower leagues a little bit harder, as ability is a factor in how players now adapt to tactics and roles, but if you keep things simple and make the right signings, you will find that this save can be a lot of fun for the first few seasons. Making the jump to the football leagues, however, is a different ball game, and that's where the real challenge kicks in.

‘The past is not going to help’ – Pochettino urges Man Utd players to look ahead

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mauricio Pochettino has called on his Manchester United to start "building our own way" as "the past is not going to help" them.

The former Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea boss was appointed as the Red Devils' new head coach after the dismissal of Michael Carrick.

Ahead of Pochettino's first match in charge, which is a trip to Anfield on Sunday, United sit in the Premier League relegation zone after 10 games.

United are winless in eight Premier League matches (D5 L3), the joint-longest ongoing run of any side this season, along with Hull City. The Red Devils last had a longer winless league run between November 1989 and February 1990 (11 in a row).

This is the latest into a league season (MD11) that Liverpool and United have faced with one of the sides in the relegation zone since 1972-73, when bottom side United beat league-leaders Liverpool 2-0 at Old Trafford in November.

Pochettino has urged his United players to forget about their poor form as they look to make a winning start under the Argentinian this weekend.

"I don't think too much about the past. It is about building our own way here. Of course, we feel the support from the club and we start to work," said Pochettino.

"The past is not going to help, to think about yesterday. We have been speaking to the players and the most important thing is to think about the next action.

"We feel the support and we are going to work really hard to help the players to perform. That is the priority."

Working towards Sunday  https://t.co/T2Sr8oRSPq

— Manchester United (@ManUtd) November 20, 2026

Pochettino will become the first-ever United boss to see his first league game with the club come against rivals Liverpool.

Just one of the Red Devils' last nine managers (including caretakers) has lost his first Premier League game of a spell in charge of the club (W4 D4), with Erik ten Hag losing 1-2 to Brighton in August 2022.

"When we arrive, I think we found a group of players that we propose to do the football that we feel," added Pochettino.

"To be brave and be Manchester United. It is about proposing that idea to be brave and take it to the field.

"We are here not to denounce things. We are here to build something with our players.

"I think it is a race to arrive and be competitive against Liverpool. That is where our focus is 100%."

White and Vancouver out to ‘finish the job’ in MLS Cup playoffs

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Brian White has made it abundantly clear that he and the Vancouver Whitecaps are out to avenge their near-miss in the MLS Cup playoffs last season in 2026.

The Whitecaps lost to Inter Miami in last season's MLS Cup final, which was their first ever appearance in the showcase match, but they have bounced back superbly.

Jesper Sorensen's side enjoyed their best MLS regular season, winning the Western Conference, with their points tally (64) and total wins (19) both higher than their previous best, set in 2025 (63 points, 18 wins).

Vancouver are also looking to become just the second Canadian side to win the MLS Cup, after Toronto FC's success back in 2017.

They will also hope to follow the template set by Toronto that season too, as the last team to lose an MLS Cup final in one season (2016 vs Seattle Sounders) and win it the next (2017 vs the same opponents).

"Losing finals is never easy," White told MLSoccer.com this week. "Then to go through two of them and to lose both is obviously a very difficult thing to process and deal with.

"But as players and as professionals, I think it's even more motivating to want to get back to those spots and, in a sense, redeem yourselves and finish the job.

"That's what everyone was focused on going into this season, was getting back to the MLS Cup, and winning it."

LOCKED IN FOR SALT LAKE Our Round 1 opponent is set @realsaltlake BC Place Sunday, Nov 22 @ 6:30 PM PCT https://t.co/tWrPgUnaPx https://t.co/YR7yt7vr4F Swipe for the full schedule #OwnTheSummit https://t.co/aEcRFXB2QS

— Vancouver Whitecaps FC (@WhitecapsFC) November 20, 2026

The Whitecaps had endured a difficult stretch as early as last month, going four MLS matches without a win (D3 L1) before eventually finishing the season with straight victories.

"We had ups and downs, especially after the World Cup. We struggled a bit with our results," said Vancouver midfielder Thomas Muller.

"In the beginning, I had the feeling we wouldn't struggle with our playing style. We were kind of dominant but not really effective.

"In between, after that, we also sometimes struggled with the games we played. But now we are on the upslope.

“And maybe teams adapted a bit to us. We were at the top of the table almost the whole season, and they looked a little bit more into it: How can we hurt them?

"How can we maybe avoid their strengths? And maybe that's also what happened in one or two games. But we are always a team that kind of fights back."

Vancouver will face Real Salt Lake this weekend after Pablo Mastroeni's side overcame the Seattle Sounders 4-2 on penalties following a 1-1 draw in normal time.

Albert Rusnak opened the scoring in the 36th minute, having missed a penalty five minutes earlier, before Morgan Guilavogui brilliantly scored the equaliser just after the hour.

Rafael Cabral came to RSL's rescue in the shoot-out, saving penalties from Rusnak and Dejan Joveljic to seal his team's place in the next round.

White is Vancouver's main threat. He scored a career-best 20 goals in the regular season, his fourth straight season with 15 or more goals.

He is the fourth player in MLS history with four straight regular seasons with 20+ goals, joining Bradley Wright-Phillips (five), Denis Bouanga and Robbie Keane.

If it had not been for Cabral's heroics against Seattle, RSL would have found themselves putting their feet up. Instead, they are on their way to BC Place.

Cabral made five saves, his joint-most in a match this season (also five against Inter Miami), while also keeping out three penalties, two in the shoot-out and one in the match.

RSL have four wins, three draws, and 11 losses on their travels this season in league matches, and will need to win away at Vancouver for the first time since February 2023, when Justen Glad and Damir Kreilach were both on target, to give themselves a chance.
